Quiet, Can You Hear It?
Created on October 3rd, 2025
Quiet, can you hear it now?
The whisper underneath the noise,
A gentle breeze against your brow,
A soft and steady, tender voice.

It speaks of all the things you are,
Not loud enough to shake the sky,
But constant as the farthest star,
A truth that doesn't need to fly.

It's in the space between your tears,
The pause before you catch your breath,
The stillness that has held your fears,
The quiet life that conquers death.

Can you hear your heart at rest?
The beauty in the silent spring,
The strength you carry in your chest,
The song that only you can sing.

It doesn't roar, it doesn't shout,
It settles like the winter snow,
A knowing deep within, throughout—
The quiet you already know.

So listen to the voice inside,
The one that's always been your home,
It whispers where the truth can hide:
You've never really been alone.
The Love That Remains
Created on October 3rd, 2025
I loved you, but the love I gave,
Was not a chain that makes a slave,
It was a seed I chose to sow,
In soil where I hoped it would grow.

I loved you, but I love me more,
Than I had ever loved before,
I love the person I must be,
The one you never stopped to see.

I loved you, but my heart's not stone,
It learned the way to stand alone,
It found its rhythm in the ache,
And learned the difference hearts can make.

I loved you, but the chapter's turned,
A lesson tenderly unlearned,
The love I gave was not in vain,
It now becomes a falling rain.

That waters roots I'm coming to,
A stronger, more enduring truth,
For what I feel for my own soul,
Is making finally, finally whole.
Echoes in the Green Tangle
Created on October 2nd, 2025
A Tribute to Jane Goodall

In the tangle of green where the tall trees all lean,
Young Jane dreamed of chimps with their whoops and their gleams.
But oh, how they hollered and harried her days,
Stealing her sketches right out of her gaze.

She'd perch on a stump with her pencil held tight,
To capture their gambols in soft morning light.
Yet Fifi would filch it, that sly silver thief,
And scribble wild loops on a termite-leaf wreath.

Then Goliath would grumble, his belly a-roar,
And raid her poor lunch till there wasn't no more.
Bananas and berries, all gulped in a go,
Leaving Jane with just echoes of hooting below.

The little ones scampered, with paws like small storms,
Tugging her braids till they frayed into forms.
They'd climb up her shoulders and chatter her ear,
With questions that tumbled like raindrops of fear.

Oh, the jungle kept calling with whispers so sweet,
But each joyful tussle brought aches to her feet.
Till one twilight hush, as the shadows grew long,
Jane saw in their eyes what she'd known all along:

The swings that they shared were as fleeting as breath,
A tender tangle twixt living and death.
She hugged Fifi close in the firefly's glow,
And whispered, "My dears, we must let some things go."
The Early Bird Considers the Worm
Created on October 1st, 2025
They say the Dawn—rewards the Keen—
But what of Those who slumber on?
The Worm, unearthed before its Time,
Might wish it had—stayed Home—

I watched a Robin, proud with Prey,
While Sluggards dreamt in Beds of Down—
Yet something in that writhing Prize
Made Victory—seem less a Crown—

The Early Riser claims the Prize—
But who would be the Worm?
Perhaps the Glory lies—
In knowing when to turn—

To rise—or rest—what Wisdom says
The Conqueror has won the Day?
When Dawn breaks cold—and Beds are warm—
I think I'd rather—stay.
The Whisper of the Pines
Created on October 1st, 2025
The forest hums with secrets none can name,
Its pine trees sway beneath a silver sky.
Their needles brush the air with soft lament,
As if to speak of lives that linger here.
The path winds slow through shadows deep and still,
Where moss clings low to granite’s weathered face.
Each step I take disturbs the quiet earth,
Yet nature holds no grudge for human tread.

The brook nearby runs cold with mountain snow,
Its murmur weaves a thread through ancient stone.
I pause to watch the light on water dance,
A fleeting gift before the dusk descends.
The trees stand guard, their roots entwined with time,
Each ring a tale of winters overcome.
What man could know the weight of seasons borne,
When all his days are but a fleeting breath?

The wind grows sharp, it cuts through woolen coat,
Yet warmth persists in how the land endures.
The field beyond lies open to the stars,
Its grasses bow to night’s unyielding call.
I stand and listen to the pines’ low chant,
Their voices blend with dreams of those long gone.
This land outlives our striving and our pain,
Its quiet heart beats on where ours may fade.
The Dragon's Spotted Dance
Created on September 30th, 2025
A dragon with a coat of dots 
Begins to dance on grassy spots. 
He hears a tune the wind has played 
Beneath the sun and moon's arcade. 

His polka is a happy sound 
That makes the trees sway all around. 
The spotted wings begin to beat 
In time with his four-clawed feet. 

A badger peeks out from his den 
And joins the dance, then goes again. 
The forest holds its breath in awe 
To witness this unwritten law. 

That magic is not fierce or grim,
But found in such a joyful whim.

The Brook's Secret Song
Created on September 30th, 2025
A brook that chatters to the stones
In water-whispers, sings alone,
And tells of where the river goes
Beneath the arch where no one knows.

It speaks of fish with silver scales
And of the wind that tells it tales,
Of mossy banks and dragonflies
That watch with great and golden eyes.

So if you hear a liquid word
From that fast-flowing, small stream heard,
You'll know the secret it has sung
Since all the world was fresh and young.
Mommy! Where did the stars go?
Created on September 26th, 2025
Last night she saw the stars for her first time—
Those silver points that pierce the summer night
Above our meadow where the fireflies
Dance lazy circles through the cooling air.
She'd never stayed awake past nine before,
But bedtime prayers had stretched to wonder long
At how the heavens hold such tiny lights
Without them falling like the autumn rain.

Her small hand pressed against the window glass,
She counted seven, then gave up the count
When more kept blooming in the deeper blue.
"They're beautiful," she whispered, eyes grown wide—
I thought she'd drink the whole night sky complete
And carry starlight in her sleeping dreams.

But morning came the way it always does:
The sun climbed over Johnson's pasture hill
And painted gold across our kitchen floor.
She woke and ran to that same window sill
Where she had watched her first night's wonder grow,
Then turned to me with panic in her voice:
"Mommy! Where did the stars go? Are they dead?"

How do you tell a child that certain things
Hide but never leave? That stars are patient,
Waiting behind the blue we think is void,
Like seeds beneath the snow that trust the warmth?
I lift her to the sill and point above
Where Polaris keeps its faithful guard,
Unseen but constant as the morning tide.

"They're sleeping now," I say, "the way you slept.
Tonight they'll wake and light their lamps again."
She nods, content this simple truth feels right—
How stars need rest behind the light of day
To shine their brightest when the darkness falls.
Morning on the Shore
Created on September 25th, 2025
The morning breaks its cloudy veil,
The sky turns soft and bright.
The shore, a bride in dawn’s new day,
First light that clears the night.

A seal leaps through rolling waves,
Its arcs catch dawn’s first flame.
The sea’s low hum, like whispered staves,
Speaks truths without a name.

A seagull’s shrill cry splits the air,
It wheels above the foam.
The tide rolls gently, free of care,
As if it knows its home.

What pulls the heart to watch this scene?
The light that shifts and plays?
The dolphins dance where waves convene,
In morning’s golden haze.

I stand on sand, the world’s renewed,
The shore feels wide and free.
The sea’s vast pulse, so long pursued,
Says more than I can see.

Dusk on the Shore
Created on September 25th, 2025
The ocean hums beneath the fading sun,
Its waves roll in, unhurried, to the shore.
The light is soft, a glow of amber flame,
That dances on the water’s endless face.

A gull swoops down, its wings cut through the dusk,
Its call a thin, sharp note against the tide.
The crabs move slow, their claws like tiny flags,
As if they mark the edge of sea and land.

What is it in the sea that holds the mind?
Its ceaseless motion, or its hidden depths?
The dolphins weave through the surf, their bodies sleek,
Like shadows playing in the dying light.

I stand upon the beach, my feet in sand,
And feel the pull of something vast and old.
The sun slips low, its fire now nearly gone,
And stars begin to speckle out the sky.

The tide keeps on, its rhythm never still,
As if it speaks a language I can’t learn.
A fish breaks free, a fleeting arc of silver,
Then dives back to the dark from which it came.

The dusk grows deep, the world feels strange and wide.
I turn for home, the salt still on my lips.
Refreshed
Created on September 24th, 2025
I walked the woods this morning after rain
Had washed the summer's dust from every leaf
And left the oaks glistening in light
That slanted gold through branches overhead.
The air was crisp—October's breath had come
To whisper warnings that the year grew old,
Though warmth still lingered in the sheltered spots
Where ferns unfurled their fronds like open hands.

A chipmunk chattered somewhere in the oak,
His winter stores not quite sufficient yet
For what he sensed was coming with the frost.
I stopped to watch him dart from tree to tree,
His cheeks bulged full with acorns he had found,
And thought how we're alike in autumn's pull—
Both gathering what we think might see us through
The barren months that wait beyond the hill.

The path curved down beside a stone-wall line
Where someone generations past had cleared
These fields and piled the granite shoulder-high.
Now birches grew where corn once stood in rows,
And what had been a pasture forty years
Before was thick with pine and juniper.
The wall remained, though purposeless it seemed,
A testament to labor no one needs.

I wondered at the man who built this wall,
How many seasons watched him lift each stone
And fit it to its place with calloused hands,
What dreams he had of permanence and home.
The woods reclaim whatever we let go—
First weeds, then saplings, then the forest floor
Returns to cover up our small intent,
And all our planning comes to this: a wall.

The morning mist was rising from the stream
That wandered through the valley down below.
I could have followed it, but chose instead
To climb the ridge and see what lay beyond.
Sometimes the path we take decides itself—
Not by our will, but by the way we're bent
By seasons we have weathered in our time,
By walls we've built, and walls we've let decay.
Evening by the Falls
Created on September 22nd, 2025
The water tumbles hard from cragged stone,
A boy, alone, I stand where mist ascends,
The evening light through spruce and pine is thrown,
Its golden haze where falling water bends.

The wind hums low, a rustle in the leaves,
Like whispers of a choice I’ll soon regret—
To chase the rainbow’s arc that water weaves,
Or hold my place where firmer paths are set.

The birds pipe clear, their notes a fleeting call,
While insects drone in circles near the spray.
I watch the colors dance where waters fall,
And wonder if I’ll lose myself today.

The woods are deep, their shadows cool and dense,
Each step I take could lead to joy or cost.
The falls keep singing, wild with eloquence,
Their voice a pull toward something nearly lost.

I pause, my heart half-caught by what I see—
The fleeting gleam, the mist, the endless sound.
To wander free or turn back sensibly?
The falls don’t care which path my feet have found.
Song of the Solitary Self
Created on September 19th, 2025
I sing the electric song of singular souls,
Each breath a universe unfolding in morning mist,
Where ancient oaks whisper secrets to wandering hearts,
And every footstep marks a path never before taken,
The earth beneath acknowledging each unique pressure,
Each stride a statement written in dust and dew.

Look! There walks a woman gathering wild berries,
Her fingers stained purple with summer's abundance,
She alone knows the sweetness of this particular moment,
The weight of sunlight on her shoulders hers entirely,
No other soul can taste what her tongue discovers,
No other eyes can see her exact shade of joy.

And here a child builds castles from river stones,
Each tower leaning at angles known only to him,
The water rushing past carries away nothing essential,
For what matters lives in the choosing, the placing,
The fierce concentration of small hands creating
A kingdom that exists because he wills it so.

Watch how the old man tends his vegetable garden,
Bent but unbroken, teaching tomatoes to climb,
His weathered palms hold conversations with soil
That began before words, before names were needed,
Each plant responds to his particular touch,
Growing crooked and beautiful under his care.

The mountain stands witness to all our becoming,
Silent observer of a million individual dawns,
Each sunrise painting the peaks in colors
That have never been exactly this way before,
And will never be again, though seasons circle,
Though the earth spins its ancient, faithful dance.

I contain multitudes, yes, but so does the sparrow
Picking breadcrumbs from the café table at dawn,
So does the teenager sketching in her notebook,
Dreaming futures only she can imagine,
Her pencil moving to rhythms inside her chest,
Creating worlds that pulse with her particular blood.

We are each the center of our own expanding universe,
Stars wheeling around the gravity of our choices,
The wind carries our separate songs across meadows
Where wildflowers lean toward their own understanding
Of light, of growth, of the wild necessity
To bloom exactly as they are meant to bloom.

So I celebrate the unrepeatable miracle of you,
Standing distinct among seven billion others,
Your thoughts branching like rivers toward unknown seas,
Your heart beating its singular, irreplaceable rhythm,
While around us the world offers its eternal invitation:
Be yourself, completely, without apology or fear.
Author's Notes: I became concerned over a year ago about the loss of individuality. Today we seem to see people as we, them, Democrat, Republican,  not as he, she. The problem with doing this is we loose compassion for each other. You will see a lot of Walt Whitman in this poem, because I enjoy reading his poetry and as I was reading his work I would get an idea and run to quickly write down an idea. Today is the culmination of what took almost a year to write. It's long, but I hope it resonates with someone.
A Home Recipe
Created on September 19th, 2025
Three cups of patience, finely sifted,
Two teaspoons of care,
A tablespoon of kindness gifted
Measured with a prayer.

Add dashes of forgiveness pure,
A pound of gentle touch,
Fold laughter in to make it sure—
Never add too much.

Then bake with time at steady heat,
Season well with grace,
The recipe makes love complete
For all who find this place.

Nature's Solitary Garden
Created on September 19th, 2025
The garden sleeps in winter's grip— 
    Each bloom lies still and cold,
While love once warm begins to slip 
    Through fingers, growing old. 

The oak stands stark against the sky,
    Its branches bare and lone,
As seasons pass and mortals die 
    In silence, on their own. 

Yet in this hush of frost and stone 
    A tender voice rings clear— 
Though death may claim what we have known,
    Love lingers, drawing near. 

The earth will wake, the buds will break 
    From winter's icy hold,
And what seemed lost for sorrow's sake 
    Returns in green and gold.
Go Seek
Created on September 17th, 2025
With small hands pressed over your eyes,
you counted slowly— 
footsteps scattered like leaves,
then silence. 

You search through gardens, closets,
shadowed halls where laughter 
echoed through summer dusk,
each discovery 
a burst of joy blooming again. 

Every hiding place became sacred: 
behind curtains,
underneath stairs,
where breathing slowed to whispers,
heartbeats 
stilled in perfect stillness. 

But the game grew subtle— 
seasons turned,
no longer bound by childhood's simple rules. 
You learned to hide from questions,
glances,
touch. 

Now they call your name 
across crowded squares,
but corners 
hold you fast with quiet arms. 
These spaces,
small enough for one alone,
have taught you silence 
as their only gift. 

Yet somewhere— 
in this vast and peopled earth— 
another player moves through shadow-work,
their footsteps soft 
on paths you recognize,
breathing matched to rhythms 
you have always known. 

Until that moment
when someone finds your corner,
kneels down beside you
in the quiet space,
and suddenly
the hiding feels too small
for hearts that finally
dare to be complete.
The Weight of Carrying a Secret
Created on September 17th, 2025
Beneath my ribs—a stone takes residence
With edges sharp against my breathing wall,
Each swallow carrying lead through narrow paths
While shoulders bow beneath this silent mass.

The weight grows dense with morning's first approach
As consciousness returns to find it there—
This burden wrapped in flesh and sinew tight,
A gravity that pulls my spine toward earth.

My chest constricts around its jagged truth,
The secret pressing outward from within—
But why do I hold fast to what burns through
My lungs like captured smoke, like borrowed flame?

The muscle memory of carrying loads
Knows nothing of this phantom architecture:
How words unspoken gather substance here
And transform to iron in my hollow spaces.

At night it shifts—settles deep in my bones,
A fever without heat, an ache unnamed.
My body bends around what can't be told,
Creating caverns where the silence dwells.

Yet still I bear this contraband of thought—
This freight of knowledge wrapped in flesh and time.
What makes us choose to house what wounds us so?
Until I am the vessel it requires.
The Rusted Scythe
Created on September 17th, 2025
Beneath the weighted branches of the oak,
Where silence wraps itself in winter's breath,
A rusted scythe lies buried in the drift—
Its blade forgotten by the turning world.

The steel remembers seasons long dissolved,
When summer grass surrendered to its edge,
And farmer's hands would guide it through the field
While larks rose singing from the morning ground.

Now frost has claimed the handle's weathered wood,
And snow obscures the metal's ancient curve.
No harvest calls, no wheat stands proud and gold—
Just emptiness that stretches past the trees.

I watch this tool of labor left behind,
A witness to the rhythm of decay.
The woods accept what human purpose dropped,
And winter covers all without regard.

Yet something stirs within this quiet scene:
The blade endures though seasons overwhelm.
Perhaps in spring the snow will melt away
And earth will show what time cannot destroy.
A Love Letter on a Winter Morning
Created on September 16th, 2025
The frost has etched its script on windowpanes,
A cold that grips the heart and clouds the breath.
I hold the letter, frail as winter's grace,
Its ink a wound that bleeds beneath my touch.

The snow falls soft, a shroud on buried hopes,
Each flake a fleeting thought of what was lost.
My anger stirs, a ember in the chill,
Yet sorrow binds it like a chain of ice.

This page, once warm with promises, now fades,
Its words a ghost that haunts the morning's gloom.
I trace the lines where love and rage entwined,
And wonder if the fault was hers or mine.

The dawn is gray, no sun to thaw the soul,
The letter trembles in my tightened grip.
What use is wrath when grief outlives its flame?
The cold endures; the heart learns to forget.

I fold the note and let the silence speak,
Its quiet deeper than my heart's lament.
The winter morning holds me in its thrall,
And sorrow carves its name where anger falls.
Wearing the Echo
Created on September 14th, 2025
I wore my name like borrowed thread—
It fit but didn't cling.
I spoke in tones I thought were mine
But didn't feel them sing.

The days were clocks I watched them tick,
Each hour a quiet room.
I filled them up with practiced steps
And left before they bloomed.

Then you arrived—not loud, but sure,
A knock I didn't plan.
You asked me why the curtains closed
And touched the light I ran.

You didn't change the way I spoke
But taught me how to hear
The echo I mistook for truth
Was silence dressed in fear.

I do not mourn the me I was—
She did the best she knew.
But now I wear my name with ease
And walk in colors true.

You were not map—you were the road,
Not mirror but the face.
And I, who thought I knew the way,
Now travel into grace.

A Mother's Lament
Created on September 14th, 2025
The toys are where they left them yesterday.
A block half-built, a bear without its ear.
The light comes in and doesn’t ask to stay—
it moves across the wall, then disappears.
I sit and watch the silence fill the space,
not empty, but no longer quite the same.

He used to hum before he knew the words.
She used to laugh at things that weren’t a joke.
They’d name the moon, then ask if it was mine,
and I would lie, because that’s what we do.
We give them myths to hold before they learn
how much the truth can weigh when held too long.

The floor still creaks beneath my careful step.
I walk around the places they once fit.
A crib becomes a shelf, a shelf a desk.
The walls don’t mind—they’ve seen it all before.
But I do. I remember every mark
they made without intending it to last.

There’s something in the way a child forgets
that teaches us the art of letting go.
They move ahead without a backward glance,
while we stay back and name the things they dropped.
A sock, a phrase, a drawing of a cloud—
each one a kind of map we cannot read.

I do not wish to hold them where they were.
I only wish the echo stayed awhile.
The way they said “tomorrow” like a place,
or asked if dreams could happen while awake.
I answered once. I don’t recall the words.
But something in me still believes they’re true.

The River After Rain
Created on September 13th, 2025
The river swells like a held breath released,
dark water surging past the dock
where I carved my initial
in the summer’s lazy grammar.
Today the current carries
a broken branch, a rusted can—
oil slicks shimmer where fish swam.

You stand beside me, my shadow of wonder, silent.
I know you’re fading.
Your light dims like fireflies in smog,
here, then gone.
Did I trade you for screens, for noise?
The river’s roar accuses stone—and me.

I want to call you back,
but words are minnows
slipping through my fingers,
silver and impossible to hold.
I wade in, hands grasping at sludge,
but the current laughs, cold.

The rain returns, soft,
then drums the planks
like fingers thumping on a table, impatiently.
A drone’s hum drowns the river’s song.

I am one boy and his ghost watching water rise,
knowing it will carry away
the old pier, the rope swing,
the name I gave this place—
Willow Bend—like it held my secrets.
What do we keep when the world forgets its own?
The River That Builds and Destroys
Created on September 13th, 2025
The River That Builds

It starts as questions, small and bright,
That spark in minds by candlelight—
Each answer weaves a thread of lore,
Each query opens one more door
To truths that time cannot ignore.

The child who asks "but why?" today
Becomes the one who lights the way—
From Galileo's stars to freedom's rings,
From marches claiming equal things,
They build the world that freedom brings.

Yet even here, a shadow creeps—
Curiosity, unchecked, can leap
Too far, crafting tools too vast,
Like splitting atoms in the past,
Whose power heals, or burns too fast.

The River That Destroys

It starts with fear, a whispered claim,
That seeks a face to pin the blame—
The stranger's voice, the different creed,
A scapegoat for a growing need
To crush what stirs our inner greed.

From whispers grow the urgent cries,
Like Salem's trials or Red Scare lies,
Till crowds converge with blinded aim,
And reason falters in the flame,
Yet hope may flicker through the blame.

Yet even here, a spark of care—
A doubt that questions what's unfair—
Can twist toward light, though frail and small,
Like voices breaking Berlin's wall,
Where hope and ruin rise and fall.

The Choice

Both rivers rise from one deep spring:
The human heart's unending spring.
They escalate where motives blend—
No path is pure, no stream's sure end,
Yet still we choose which tide to tend.

The fertile plain or storm's cruel wrath?
Each step we take defines the path.
When pain tempts hate to steer the flow,
Love fights to pull us from below—
A struggle carved in every woe.

No easy choice, no certain gain,
For human hearts bear both love and pain.
Each step we take, the tide will swell,
To shape a world we cannot tell—
Its rise or fall, no heart can spell.
Palimpsest
Created on September 12th, 2025
They wrote in ink, but not to last—
Each word a veil, each phrase a mask.
Beneath the script, the ghost remains,
A deeper text in phantom stains.

The rose in verse, a thorn concealed—
Its petals speak, but wounds revealed.
The raven's cry in midnight dread,
Unspoken lore in feathers spread.

The parchment bears a thousand hands,
Each scraping truth to meet demands.
A symbol veiled, a truth confined—
Yet underneath, the soul's design.

We learn to read the buried scroll,
The tremor tucked inside "control,"
The way a pause can split a vow,
Or silence mark the sacred now.

The sonnet’s turn, a breath withheld,
The haiku’s hush where grief is spelled—
All layered in a quiet war,
Of meaning lost and metaphor.

Words fade like breath in winter's frost,
Their echoes fleeting, quickly lost.
But feelings carve a lasting groove,
Their silent weight no time can move.

For language is a borrowed skin,
Where deeper stories press within.
And those who learn to look askew
May find their own reflection too.

The Grotesque Feast of Grizelda Grub
Created on September 11th, 2025
In the village of Slimington-on-Grime,
Lived Grizelda Grub, who thought it sublime
To collect all things vile, putrid, and gross,
The things that make normal folks hold their nose!

"Oh wonderful!" Grizelda would shriek with delight,
As maggots came crawling into her sight,
She'd scoop them up gently in bowls made of rust,
Mixed with some toenails and gray basement dust.

Her pantry was stocked with delectable treats:
Jars full of earwax and moldy pig's feet,
Pickled eyeballs that stared back at you,
And bottles of bile—a sickening brew!

For breakfast she'd feast on some curdled milk soup,
With lumps that would jiggle and wiggle and droop,
She'd slurp it up loudly with slobbering sounds,
While rats in her kitchen went racing around.

Her lunch was a sandwich of fungus and slime,
With mustard that bubbled and fizzed all the time,
The bread was so moldy it grew its own hair,
And smelled like a skunk mixed with underwear!

But dinner—oh dinner!—was Grizelda's best meal,
She'd cook up a stew that would make strong folks reel:
With boogers and belly-button lint for the base,
And snot from the nose of a walrus's face!

She'd add rotten cabbage that oozed purple goo,
Some socks that had festered inside someone's shoe,
A handful of beetles, still squirming about,
And fish scales that made the whole mixture sprout!

So if you should meet her while walking one day,
Just smile and be polite, then quickly walk away,
For those who stay longer might just get invited,
To dinner—where normal stomachs get frightened!

And though her cuisine makes most people quite queasy,
Old Grizelda proves that being gross can be easy,
She lives life with gusto, with slime, and with grime—
The queen of revolting, disgusting, gross rhyme!

Knock-Knock at Hollow House
Created on September 10th, 2025
I wore a jester’s cap and tail—
A black cat stitched from midnight’s veil.
Rachael, raccoon, in witch’s guise,
Had candle-flicker in her eyes.

And Freddie—fox beneath a sheet—
Made silence echo at his feet.
We tiptoed past the lanterns’ leer,
Each pumpkin carved to mock our fear.

The house loomed like a whispered sin,
Its windows blinked with bone-white grin.
I knocked—a sound, a knuckle’s crack—
Sent whispers crawling down my back.

No voice replied, but curtains swayed—
Like breath from something long decayed.
A mirror flashed—a face, then two—
Not ours, but something passing through.

Its smile was mine, but stretched too wide—
Like laughter drowned in formaldehyde.
We fled. The woods grew teeth and tongue.
The path behind us came undone.

And though no soul had said hello,
That night, my dreams began to glow.
Its voice returned in whispered rhyme—
“You knocked too late. Come back next time.”

Sanctuary of Twilight
Created on September 10th, 2025
The sky’s amber bleeds through the wound of the day,
As the ocean reflects all the light that’s to fade,
Each wave whispers soft as a prayer’s gentle sway,
To the hush of the night in its sacred cascade.

In this cradle of sand where the sea softly flows,
I’m the stillness the world in its rush left behind,
A seed sown in silence where vastness bestows,
Where time’s tangles unravel and peace I can find.

The breeze brushes through me like love’s tender breath,
Salt-kissed as a mother’s sweet song in the air,
While gulls trace their hymns through the sky’s boundless depth,
Holding me in its strength with a beauty so fair

These colors might be how the divine chooses speech—
Rose gold with the copper and purple entwined,
A canvas that stretches from peak unto peak,
A vision of splendor for my eyes alone signed.

The waves stroke the shore as they lull it to rest,
Their rhythm my heartbeat now slowing with ease,
As earth breathes in quiet, its pulse manifest,
I’m both silence and sound in this moment’s release.

This instant’s a church with no walls to confine,
Each grain of the sand holds a prayer I have spun,
Where peace falls like evening in soft, gentle line,
And fear finds its rest when the day is undone.

I witness this hour when the day yields to night,
In surrender I find there’s a power to claim,
To be both the dark and the glow of the light,
In twilight’s embrace where I’m whole once again.

The Museum of Becoming
Created on September 9th, 2025
The mirror swallows your same face each morning—
your teeth, your eyes, the scar above your lip
like a comma in an unfinished sentence.
Your coffee cup holds its familiar weight, steam spiraling
the same tired dance while newspapers yellow
in neat stacks, each headline a door
you never opened.

Clock hands crawl like injured spiders
across their web of numbered hours.
Tuesday bleeds into Wednesday bleeds
into a month of Wednesdays until
the calendar becomes a graveyard
of crossed-out promises, crossed-out
days.

But somewhere between sleep and waking,
the house has grown new shadows.
Your childhood bedroom—a museum
of someone else's dreams. Photographs
on the mantle stare back
with stranger's eyes: your mother's laugh lines
carved deeper now, like riverbeds
in drought.

The tree outside your window stands
where it always stood, yet its branches
reach toward different stars. Your hands,
moving through familiar motions, belong
to someone who learned to hold loss
like water cupped in trembling
palms.

When did the mirror learn
to lie? When did your footsteps
start echoing in hallways you thought
you knew? The walls remember
a different voice calling goodnight
to ghosts who no longer
answer.

Time moves like mist through open fingers—
imperceptible as the lengthening of hair,
quiet as dust accumulating
on books you swore you'd read
again. Until one day you turn
around and find yourself standing
in the ruins of who you used
to be, surrounded by the archaeology
of a life lived one forgotten Wednesday
at a time.

Take Me Instead
Created on September 6th, 2025
I am the hinge between hunger and hope,
my body a comma in the forest's long sentence of survival.

Behind me, folded like a prayer in ferns,
my fawn breathes in whispered beats—
each exhale a secret I would die to keep,
a promise buried deep.

But you, golden shadow, muscle wrapped in silence,
you have found us with your amber mathematics,
calculating the distance between want and taking,
your patience breaking like dawn.

So I step forward into the cathedral of your gaze,
each hoof-fall a bell ringing: here, here, here—
not my child, but me,
not the hidden, but the free.

Look at me.

I make myself a constellation of motion,
tail flagging white against the dark devotion
of your hungry stare—
shadow-patterns dancing on my spine like warnings,
like prayers offered to the mourning air.

I am the decoy moon pulling your tide toward me,
away from the small sun sleeping in speckled fur,
away from innocence that must not stir.

See how I stumble—see how my leg
trembles with imagined weakness,
how I beg without words for your attention's weight,
how I create this theater of fate.

Your focus becomes a chain I wear,
heavier than winter, sharper than despair.
It presses against my ribs until my heart speaks
in a language older than words:

Take me instead.

I am the sacrifice that walks on delicate legs,
the offering that breathes with deliberate raggedness,
drawing your predator-prayer away from innocence—
my love a fierce defense.

In this moment, I am not doe—
I am pure attention made flesh and bone,
a living target painted with love's geometry,
a mother's desperate artistry.

Behind me, my fawn stays hidden,
a small god dreaming of grass and safety
while I dance with death's golden eyes,
while I weave these necessary lies.

Knowing that sometimes the greatest gift we give
is to be seen when someone else must live
unseen, untouched by predator's desire—
I draw you toward my willing fire.

Watch me. Follow me.
Let that be enough.
Let love be enough.

The Claptrap Cabinet
Created on September 6th, 2025
In the dusty corner of my grandmother's attic stands
A peculiar contraption she calls the Claptrap—
Not filled with empty words or pompous plans,
But literally designed to catch applause and snap

Each clap between its wooden jaws—
She built it during wartime rationing days
When praise was scarce and approval laws
Demanded we save every cheer that pays.

The cabinet creaks with captured ovations:
My first piano recital's nervous clapping,
Uncle Ted's golf stories' false adorations,
The polite applause when Cousin May was rapping.

Some claps are genuine—bright and clean,
Others hollow as campaign promises,
A few are sarcastic (you know what I mean),
And some are just social compromises.

When loneliness visits on quiet nights,
She opens the Claptrap's weathered door
And releases a symphony of delights—
Hands meeting hands from years before.

The irony isn't lost on me, you see:
Her Claptrap holds what claptrap lacks—
Real moments of human harmony,
Not empty words but authentic acts.

So while the world spins tales of sound and fury,
Signifying nothing in their verbose dance,
Grandmother's wooden judge and jury
Preserves each honest, fleeting chance

When people chose to bring their palms together
In genuine recognition of the moment—
Her Claptrap hoards this precious weather
Against the storm of false atonement.

Maybe we all need such a device
To separate the real from the performed,
To hold what's honest, pure, and nice
Before the world has it transformed

Into the very thing its name suggests:
Empty noise that masquerades as gold.
But in that attic cabinet rests
The truest story ever told.
Author's Notes: contest Claptrap! - Chris Bond
The World We Forget
Created on September 6th, 2025
When I say "goodnight" and draw my blinds,
Somewhere else the morning sun climbs high,
Coffee brewing in awakening minds
While I dream beneath a starlit sky.

My casual words drift through the air—
"Keep your pecker up!" I cheer a friend,
Not knowing how across the ocean there
Those same sounds make different meanings bend.

What lifts spirits here might cause offense,
What's innocent becomes obscene,
Lost in translation's consequence,
Misunderstood in spaces between.

The rubber becomes an eraser's twin,
Fanny packs turn into bum bags,
While lifts and elevators spin
The same journey with different tags.

I forget that while I'm having lunch,
You're settling down for evening tea,
That my tomorrow is your midnight hunch,
My yesterday, your tomorrow's spree.

The world spins on, a carnival of time,
Each zone a different act in play,
Where my perfect moment's pantomime
Is your ordinary middle-of-the-day.

So when I speak of "here" and "now,"
I should remember earth's great dance—
That somewhere else, somehow,
My words arrive by circumstance.

In lands where different meanings dwell,
Where night is day and day is night,
Where words I know so very well
Might dim another's inner light.

The pitfall of the self-made world:
Forgetting others share our sphere,
That as our own lives are unfurled,
A billion others persevere.

In different tongues, in different hours,
Under different skies they roam—
Yet sharing these same earthly powers
To make their corner feel like home.
The Last Magician
Created on September 5th, 2025
We build our houses card by card, each morning
a new floor added to the tower
that will not stand—yet still we climb
the shaking stairs of maybe,
clutch at railings made of smoke.

Death, the last magician, watches
from the wings, cape sewn
with galaxies and grave dirt,
shuffling his deck of heartbeats
while we applaud our own performance.

How we rehearse immortality! Practice
walking through walls that exist,
swallow swords of steel and promises,
pull rabbits from hats that hold
only darkness and the sound
of our mothers calling us home.

The final trick: one moment
you are bread becoming toast,
steam rising like a small prayer—
the next, you are absence
wearing your favorite shirt.

And oh, the beautiful wreckage
you leave scattered: half-finished
crossword puzzles bleeding ink,
books with broken spines
still whispering their stories
to empty rooms.

Your children inherit this museum
of interrupted gestures—
the way you stirred your coffee
counterclockwise, the exact angle
of your reading glasses
abandoned on the nightstand
like two moons that forgot
how to rise.

They learn the cruelest alchemy:
how love transmutes to memory,
how your laughter becomes archaeology
they excavate from old voicemails,
how grief is just love
with nowhere left to go,
so it pools in their chests
like water in a waiting cup.

Death deals the same hand
to every player, yet still we bet
our small eternities on tomorrow,
still we shuffle through our days
as if the house always wins
for everyone but us.
Author's Notes: contest New Writes - Free Verse - Adrian Bell
The Bear Who Became a Christmas Tree
Created on September 5th, 2025
Old Bruno slumbered through winter's call,
His fur grown thick, his body tall.
As snowflakes kissed his sleepy nose,
A magic spell around him rose.

His heavy paws became a single root,
And his fur transformed into a camouflage suit
His massive arms stretched wide and high,
Reaching branches toward the sky.

Where once his heart beat warm and slow,
Now twinkled lights in cheerful glow.
His gentle spirit, kind and true,
Drew families near as Christmas grew.

Children hung their wishes bright
Upon his arms with pure delight.
He sheltered gifts beneath his frame,
Though none would ever know his name.

Bruno stood through winter's night,
A guardian bathed in golden light.
His sacrifice of flesh and bone
Made every house feel like a home.

When spring returned with warmer days,
He'd dream of summer's honey rays,
Content to know his greatest gift
Was helping weary spirits lift.
Author's Notes: She/We/The ____ Turned into a Christmas Tree - PinkFaerie5 - WC 146
The Weight of Questions
Created on September 4th, 2025
What if the self is just a story 
we tell the mirror each morning,
a fiction so convincing 
we forget we are the author? 

I watch my hand write these words— 
whose will moves the pen? 
The neurons firing in sequence,
or something that observes the firing? 

Time drips forward like honey,
each moment dissolving 
into the next, yet here I sit 
convinced of my continuity. 

Was I the same person 
who woke this morning,
or am I a fresh consciousness 
inheriting yesterday's memories? 

The universe expands 
into what? More universe? 
And if it contracts tomorrow,
will meaning collapse inward too? 

I love, therefore I suffer. 
I think, therefore I doubt. 
I exist, therefore I question 
the very ground of existing. 

Perhaps wisdom lies not 
in answering but in learning 
to hold the weight 
of unanswerable questions— 

to dance with uncertainty,
to find peace in the pause 
between heartbeats, in the space 
where knowing meets unknowing. 

The cave wall flickers 
with shadows we mistake 
for truth, but what if 
the shadows are enough? 

What if the real freedom 
is not in breaking chains 
but in choosing, moment 
by moment, what to believe?
Author's Notes: contest The Unanswered: A Philosophical Poetry Challenge - Roland Hollow
The Art of Ripening Slowly
Created on September 3rd, 2025
If I were a passion fruit,
I'd wear my contradictions on my skin—
purple-brown and wrinkled,
unpretty by conventional measures,
a weathered leather purse
forgotten in someone's closet.

You'd pass me by in produce aisles,
choosing the polished apples,
the blushing peaches,
while I sit patient as a monk,
knowing what treasures
lie beneath my humble shell.

My exterior speaks of storms weathered,
of sun-baked afternoons
and rain-soaked nights,
each dimple a story,
each crease a lesson learned
in the art of ripening slowly.

But crack me open—
go ahead, be brave—
and I'll spill golden secrets,
seeds suspended in amber nectar,
a constellation of possibility
swimming in concentrated sunlight.

I taste like summer lightning,
tart as first love's kiss,
sweet as forgiveness whispered
at three in the morning.
I am the flavor that makes you
close your eyes and remember
something you never knew you'd forgotten.

My fragrance is intoxication itself,
tropical winds carrying promises,
the scent that lingers
on fingers and lips,
that follows you home
and haunts your dreams.

I am not for everyone—
my intensity overwhelms
the timid tongue.
Some find me too much,
too complex,
too unapologetically myself.

But for those who understand
that beauty isn't always
smooth and symmetrical,
that the most profound gifts
often come wrapped
in unlikely packages—

I offer everything:
the sharp awakening of truth,
the sweetness earned
through patience,
the reminder that inside
every weathered exterior
pulses a heart
worth discovering.

So if you see me there,
small and unremarkable,
don't walk away.
Take me home.
Let me teach you
what passion really tastes like.
Author's Notes: If I Were A Fruit - Hebe Manyepxa
The Racing Clock of Life
Created on September 3rd, 2025
In childhood's realm, each day stretched wide,
A summer afternoon could last for years—
Each Christmas Eve an endless tide
Of waiting, wishing, hopeful tears.
The clock moved slow as honey poured,
Each birthday felt a lifetime earned,
While eighteen loomed like some great sword
That time itself had barely turned.

Then marriage bells and cradles rocking,
Four small hands reaching for your own,
The years began their gentle mocking—
How swift the seeds of time were sown.
From eighteen's gate to fifty's door,
Life picked up pace like morning rain,
Each decade holding so much more,
Sweet chaos mixed with joy and pain.

But fifty brought a sudden shift,
As if some cosmic gear had changed—
Time became a wind-caught drift,
The calendar rearranged.
Now seventy-six and racing still,
I'm strapped inside this speeding car,
Each season bends to time's strong will,
The finish line no longer far.

The grandkids grow between two blinks,
The seasons blur in rushing streams,
While memory selects and thinks
Of scattered moments, faded dreams.
Yet in this race against the clock,
I've learned what matters in the end—
Not how fast the years unlock,
But how we love, and laugh, and mend.

So let the Indy car race on,
Let time accelerate its pace,
For in each rapidly passing dawn
Lives beauty in this human race.
The speedway of our numbered days
May blur the scenery we pass,
But love remains in all the haze—
The only thing that's built to last.
The Deletion
Created on September 2nd, 2025
I finally deleted your number today.
Not in a fury of taps and swipes,
but with the slow, sure pressure
of a stone settling on a grave.

For months, it was a tiny, digital shrine—
that string of ten digits, a slim shard of light
holding the ghost of your "hello,"
the fossil of your laugh.

My finger hovered like a hesitant witness
over the "confirm" button—a red zero,
a period at the end of a sentence
I never learned how to speak.

This was not an act of forgetting.
It is the opposite.
It is to know your name by heart now,
instead of by memory.

It is to untether you from a signal,
to set the thought of you free
from this cold, glass-and-plastic prison,
back into the wilds of my mind.

It was the smallest of funerals.
No one sent flowers.
Just me, in the silent cathedral of my kitchen,
burying you.

Miss Kaleido’s Crazy Class
Created on September 2nd, 2025
She burst into our classroom door,
With socks that shimmered off the floor.
Her dress looked like a birthday cake,
Her glasses screamed, “I’m wide awake!”

She wrote on the board with rainbow chalk,
Made the globe spin when she'd walk.
Built a graph from sticky notes,
And turned our tests into paper boats.

Taught us math with twisty rhymes,
Mixed up numbers with the times.
Wrote sideways on the whiteboard wall,
Then danced through rows to take roll call.

Asked me why my chart was bad,
I said, “I think I saw your plaid.”
She laughed and twirled and waved goodbye—
Then vanished with a chalkboard sigh.

The bell rang loud. Her desk was bare.
Just scribbled graphs and empty air.



Author's Notes: contest Quirky Teacher - PinkFaerie5 - WC 122
The Keeper
Created on September 2nd, 2025
They told me, "Put away your childish things,"
So I packed the wonder up in reasoned, tidy boxes.
I learned the weight of words, the chill that silence brings,
And walked through days bordered by clockses.

But a stubborn part, a seed they could not find,
Refused to wither in the dark and settled dust.
It held the scent of rain on hot concrete, the kind
That smells of promise, of a world reborn and just.

It kept the memory of the snail's engraved trail,
The dizzy spin of a maple's helicopter seed,
The patient art of building a leaf-boat to sail
In the river of a gutter—a singular, mighty deed.

So now I walk with two pairs of eyes.
One sees the path, the storm, the falling night.
The other watches where the lightborn insect flies
And turns the mundane world to sudden, stunning light.

I sign the forms and pay the bills on time,
My hands are scored with lines of work and care,
Yet I still taste the sun in a clementine,
And find a universe trapped in a ladybug's lair.

The grief I've known is a deep and weathered stone,
It sits inside me, permanent and cold.
But it makes the joy, when a simple joy is known,
A sharper, brighter fire to have and hold.

For the child does not forget, the man does not regret,
The trade is off; the two are woven tight.
The years give depth to what the young heart sees,
And the young heart sets the older world alight.

I am not old, not young, but something in between—
A keeper of the key, who never lost the door.
The world is serious, brittle, sharp, and keen,
And I love it all so fiercely, now, much more than before.
Author's Notes: contest Open Sky Poetry - Miraculousthemodel
Between Storm and Still Water
Created on September 1st, 2025
Beneath the surface, currents war—
The deep against the shallow tide,
While waves crash heavy on the shore
Of all I've tried to keep inside.

The horizon splits in two each dawn:
One half ablaze with lightning's wrath,
One half the peace I've never drawn
From walking down this chosen path.

In hurricane's eye, I stand alone,
Where silence holds the chaos back,
The waters rise, then turn to stone—
This calm that follows the attack.

What sailor learns to trust the sea
That drowns him in its darkest hour,
Yet lifts him up when he's set free
To feel its devastating power?

The storm is not my enemy—
It carves new channels in the sand,
Reshapes the coastline that is me,
Reveals what firm foundations stand.

Salt burns the wounds the tempest made,
But healing comes with morning's light,
When troubled waters slowly fade
And leave reflections, clear and bright.

I am the storm, I am the sea,
The captain and the drowning man,
Learning that to truly be
Means holding what I cannot plan.

So let the waves crash overhead,
Let thunder shake what needs to break,
For in the depths where I once fled,
I find the stillness storms can make.

Phoenix on the Rise (Rewrite) by RepeatedNap
Created on August 31st, 2025
The eyes of the soulless, cold and unblinking,
Their constant and hollow gaze pins me in place.
Their mouths are dank caves, endlessly drinking
The light from the air, and speaking with grace
Of nothing that matters. I break from their stare,
And run for a door that is closing forever.
I plunge through the oaks and the choking, blind air
Where fog clings like smoke from a subterranean fire.

I run without rest, for a night and a day,
Then another, and another, ‘til time came undone.
The moon and the sun have both melted away.
I am no one. I am echo. I am gone.

And no one has noticed. Of course, no one has.

At last, the black forest begins to relent.
A shadow trips forward; I fall to the floor
Of a world wet with dew, and my spirit is bent
But broken wide open. My eyes see the door.

I enter. They stop. Every face is a stone.
Their expressions of disgust are a cold, bitter rain.
They see the old filth that the wild has not known,
And they back me away from my purpose again.

The door slams. The sound is the end of the world—
The crack of the planet when Thor’s hammer falls.

I turn from that echo. My feet are now hurled
Toward a new path that beckons and calls.
A crashing grows louder—a caged, angry water—
A river that rages and claws at its banks.
My focus finds light in the midst of the slaughter,
The one spot that shines in the midst of the ranks
Of the crushing, dark waves. I take the last step,
Not to cross, but to fall. To embrace and to yield.
My arms stretch to hug the whole world in its depth,
And the strands of my hair kiss my face on the field.

I am broken to pieces: to Earth and to Fire,
To Air and the Water that wanted me free.
My head rises up from the crash of the sirens,
And I breathe in the world. And the world breathes in me.
Author's Notes: contest Rewrite My Poem - RepeatedNap - Image provided by host from Pinterest
Harbor's Bounty
Created on August 30th, 2025
Down by the weathered dock where seagulls call,
In a tavern where the salty breezes fall,
Creamy waves of white fill up my bowl,
Like morning fog that soothes a sailor's soul.

Tender potatoes drift and gently roll,
Like ocean pearls within this warming whole,
While celery dances crisp and ocean-green,
The finest marsh-born stalks I've ever seen.

Onions shimmer clear as polished glass,
Sweet as the tides that through the harbor pass,
And there beneath the surface, clams reside,
Briny bivalves gathered from the morning tide.

Smoky bacon tells its salty tale,
Of farms that meet the ocean's windy gale,
The bowl grows warm like a lighthouse in my hand,
While harbor bells ring out across the land.

Each spoonful holds the ocean's ancient song,
Rich cream embraces all, both sweet and strong,
Here where the fishermen have shared their catch,
Since wooden ships first found this perfect match.

The chowder house echoes with briny lore,
This bowl of treasures from the ancient shore—
Where sea and land dance on my thankful tongue,
Clam chowder is my favorite one.

The Teapot Taco
Created on August 30th, 2025
I'm a little taco chef, short and round,
Sizzling in my kitchen, flavor-bound.
When I get all fired up, I holler, "¡Qué bravura!"
“Tacos frescos!” — and one crisp flauta!

I can make you carnitas or carne asada too,
Pile on the cilantro, salsa verde so true.
When I see you're hungry, don't you pout,
Tip me over and tacos pour out!

I'm a little taco chef, yes it's true,
Making tasty treasures just for you.
With my little apron and chef hat cute,
Every taco I make is an absolute hoot!

A Cliffside Walk
Created on August 29th, 2025
The wind hums a sad song, a low and lonely wail,
It tugs her frayed coat under a cold, pale moon’s veil.
The path’s a thin line carved where the cliff’s edge falls,
One slip, and the dark drop would silence all her calls.

Her thoughts swirl like the wild sea crashing far below,
A storm of old hurts, of dreams she’ll never know.
She came for the quiet, for the raw and biting air,
To shed the heavy load of her heart’s deep despair.

She stops at the cliff’s rim, feels the pull of the deep,
The whisper of nothing, where all pain goes to sleep.
A life of small wounds, a love that broke and strayed,
Seems answered by the waves of the salt-soaked bay.

She shuts her weary eyes, feels hope slip and blur,
And braces for the fall, giving in to the lure.
The tale feels familiar, a sad and worn-out scene—
A shadow on the cliff, lost to the sea’s cold green.

She leans to the void, ready to let it win…
But her shoe snags a stone, and the world comes rushing in.
In that jolt of fear, her wide eyes catch the spark,
Not of endings, but of life, burning fierce in the dark.

And in that split-second, with her heart’s frantic beat,
She sees not death’s call, but a life still incomplete.
Her hand claws a root, rough and twisted with age,
A lifeline in the chaos, a tether to her rage.

She pulls back from the edge, collapses on firm ground,
And shakes with a new sound—a sob, alive, profound.
Not a cry of defeat, but a fierce, thankful roar,
For the world’s bright pulse she won’t ignore anymore.

The end wasn’t falling. The end was her fight.
The story wasn’t leaving, but clinging to the light.
A heart that gripped the root, that chose to push and strive,
Found, teetering on the edge, the fire to stay alive.

She walks back from the precipice, slow but steady and sure,
Carrying not the end, but a soul that will endure.

A Wound I Chose to Keep
Created on August 27th, 2025
I remember missing you—a clock upon the shelf,
whose weights I wind each evening to maintain my fragile self.
Its pendulum of silver, a hypnotic, swaying sigh,
marks time I cannot spend with you, yet cannot let pass by.

The cogs are forged from moments I have polished with my breath,
each tick a tiny hammer beating reminders of your death.
The chime, when it releases, is a burden I have borne,
a note that hangs like smoke within the quiet of the morn.

I carry this machinery within a velvet case,
lined with all the echoes of your voice, your touch, your face.
Its heft is not of metal, but of things I cannot leave—
the weight of all the mornings that nothing can relieve.

But I have held it for so long its constant ache
has carved a sacred hollow that my soul will not forsake.
The gears have worn smooth grooves where sorrow pools and stays,
small reservoirs of longing fed by all these counted days.

For this remembered missing is a wound I chose to keep,
a well of ancient rainwater, both bitter-sweet and deep.
So let the mechanism turn, let the silver pendulum swing—
this burden is a testament, this clockwork of remembering.

And in its constant motion, in its precisely measured pain,
I prove that some goodbyes are not an ending, but a reign.
Author's Notes: contest Prompt 431 - Elisa Wei -  "I remember missing you..."
Love in Braille
Created on August 25th, 2025
Your fingers trace the landscape of my palm,
reading stories written in the ridges
of a life lived before you arrived—
each crease a chapter, each scar
a word I never learned to speak aloud.

Love, you tell me, is not found
in the poetry of perfect vision,
but in the quiet percussion
of hearts learning each other’s rhythms
through barriers we thought beyond reach.

You map my silences like continents,
discover the raised dots of my uncertainties,
the way my breathing changes pitch
when you’re three rooms away
and absence becomes its own raised text.

In darkness, we become translators
of a language that has no alphabet—
only the grammar of skin against skin,
the calloused edge of your thumb brushing my wrist.

The syntax of sighs, the punctuation
of fingertips finding their way home.

I am learning to love in raised letters,
to read the braille of your laughter
pressed into the hollow of my throat,
to understand that the most profound truths
are written in a script only touch can decode.

Here, blindfolded by trust,
we discover that love is not about seeing
but about being seen,
not about what eyes reveal
but about the territories fingers claim

until every surface becomes a page
and we are both reader and text,
writing ourselves into existence
one careful, deliberate touch
at a time.

Veni, vidi, vici (Almost)
Created on August 25th, 2025
I came—I saw my urgent need,
A sudden, deep and crucial deed.
I saw the roll, so plump and white,
A beacon in my darkest night.

I came to conquer Nature’s call,
To stand up brave and never fall.
I saw the paper, soft and sheer,
The answer to my every fear.

I came to claim my rightful throne,
And do my business all alone.
I saw—with panic and with dread—
A single square was left. You read.

I came, I saw, I… let out cries.
I saw the cardboard, bare, despised.
I came to vanquish, but instead,
I had to waddle off to bed.

So, Caesar, keep your ancient cheer,
My victory's not even near.
I came, I saw, I came undone…
And yelled, "Which @#$% monster used ALL BUTT ONE?!"
The Ballad of Tie-Dye Max
Created on August 25th, 2025
He came in a van, a paisley haze,
To find the field of Woodstock's craze.
His ticket was lost, but he paid with a grin,
And a slightly suspicious, home-grown gift within.

He lost his ticket, shrugged, "No sweat,"
Through mud and rain, a grateful wet.
He shared his peach, a fuzzy prize,
With sun-kissed strangers, blissful eyes.

A band played loud, the earth did thrum,
He air-guitared a psychic strum.
The bass, he swore, aligned his soul,
And made his broken spirit whole.

He hugged a cop, said, "Be at peace,"
Then fought a tie-dye for release
From a patch of mud that held him tight,
A groovy, brown, and deep delight.

He talked for hours bout the vibe,
With a girl who’d danced with Lucy's tribe.
They planned a trip in her VW Bug,
Then lost the thread and gave a shrug.

Now, when it’s mentioned, Max just sighs,
A distant, hazy, fond surmise.
"I swear I heard Jimi play…
…or was that just the second day?"

The Lantern Heart
Created on August 25th, 2025
I began as a traveler
with a map full of bright highways,
confident in my chosen route
toward a destination called Success.

But life took my compass,
tore my careful plans,
left me standing at the edge
of an unmapped forest
thick with the kind of darkness
that swallows footsteps whole.

So I learned to walk by feel,
my hands becoming eyes,
fingertips reading the braille
of bark and stone.
Each stumble taught me
where the roots reach out
to trip the unwary.

In the deepest part of the wood,
where no sunlight penetrates,
I discovered something extraordinary:
mushrooms glowing pale blue
in the rotting logs,
their bioluminescent bodies
writing poems in the dark.

I found streams that sang
even when I couldn't see them,
their liquid voices leading me
toward something I'd never
put on any map—
the underground river
of my own resilience.

Months later,
when I emerged blinking
into ordinary daylight,
I carried with me
a different kind of brightness:

the knowledge of how to navigate
by internal stars,
how to find water
by listening for its song.

Now I draw maps
for other lost travelers,
marking not just the safe paths
but the beautiful detours,
the dark places where
unexpected gifts wait:

the cave of quiet courage,
the valley of deep rest,
the mountain peak of perspective
you can only reach
by first learning to fall.

My new compass points not North
but toward whatever needs
illumination—
the forgotten corners
where light pools
in puddles of possibility,

where those who thought
they'd lost everything
discover they were simply
learning to see
in a different spectrum.

I am no longer the person
who feared getting lost.
I am the cartographer
of found things,
the guide who whispers:

Yes, it's dark here.
Yes, you're afraid.
But look—do you see
how your own heart
has become a lantern?
Author's Notes: contest Light in the Dark - Lia Rose5
Coffee Shop Mathematics
Created on August 23rd, 2025
There's a mathematics to longing—
how one smile multiplies into
a thousand imagined conversations,
how a stranger's glance becomes
the cornerstone of a house
you'll never live in.

I built cathedrals from her silhouette,
sculpted forever from the way
she looked away that first time,
pretending not to notice when I caught her watching.

But she was watching, whispers the fool
who lives between my ribs,
the one who still believes in
fairy tales and coffee shop magic.

My friend's words landed like stones
in still water—she's got a boyfriend, man—
and suddenly my cathedral was just
wishful architecture.

She said "yes" before I finished asking,
claimed him like territory
I never knew was contested.
And there I stood,
a cartographer of nowhere,
holding maps to a country
that was never mine to explore.

Still, I practiced letting her go
the way you practice piano scales:
badly, with no real intention
of getting better.

Because how do you unlove
someone who never knew your name?
How do you mourn a connection
that existed only in stolen glances?

The cruelest part:
she still looks.
Even with his arm around her,
her eyes still find mine
like a habit she can't break,
like I'm still part of some story
only she knows the ending to.

So I carry this impossible weight—
the happiness of being seen
and the pain of being dismissed,
the joy of recognition
and the agony of imagination,
learning that sometimes
the most beautiful feelings
are the ones that destroy you
one glance at a time.
Author's Notes: contest Happiness in pain! - Inia
Collateral
Created on August 23rd, 2025
The envelope’s white window holds a threat,
a final number, bold and overdrawn.
A sigh deflates the hollow in my chest—
this constant debt I cannot settle on.

But deeper still, the ledger of the soul
shows greater costs, a more disastrous toll.

My confidence, a bankrupt company,
its assets sold for pennies on the dollar.
The interest on my grief compounds in me,
a weight that makes my shaking spirit smaller.

I paid hope forward in a risky loan,
and now I am left utterly alone.

The currency of joy has crashed and failed;
my emotional reserves are drained and dry.
Against my name, success has been derailed,
repossessed by some indifferent sky.

The cost of living? More than I can bear—
a tax of constant, low-grade, silent care.

So let the bank call this a failure. Fine.
This brokenness is more than a closed account.
It is a foreclosure on a design
for light, for love, for any kind of amount.

The true cost is the sound of your own voice,
whispering you have no other choice.

Yet in this ruin, where all funds are spent,
a stubborn, priceless currency remains:
the will to find what wasn't meant
for balance sheets or for a banker's gains.

The cost was high, the brokenness is real,
but even shattered glass can spark and heal.

A Workaholic's Lament - The Weak-end
Created on August 23rd, 2025
The weekend finds me at my worst—
No structure than these days can give,
I'm weak, but still, the weak-end mends.

The Monday-Friday tower I rehearsed
Now crumbles like a house that cannot live.
The weekend finds me at my worst—

A thirst for purpose, now reversed,
With no tight meeting's iron ring to give.
I'm weak, but still, the weak-end mends.

Saturday shows me, and well-dressed,
The fragile places no one knows.
The weekend finds me at my worst—

Then Sunday whispers of tomorrow's thirst,
And offers its genteel, relieving sorrow.
I'm weak, but still, the weak-end mends.

So here between the days, accurst,
I find the me that's more than do.
The weekend finds me at my worst—
I'm weak, but still, the weak-end mends.

The Inheritance of Pages
Created on August 23rd, 2025
First, the silent, solo flight—
A private, precious, quiet right.
The spine cracks open with a sigh,
A world unfolds to just one eye.
The scent of ink and aged sheet,
A friend for solitary feet.
You trace the words that authors laid,
In chosen, thoughtful, gentle shade.
You travel far on printed seas,
A whispered, personal, gentle breeze.
This is the self, the soul's own feast,
From greatest bard to humble beast.

But then, a smaller hand finds yours,
And different magic now endures.
The same old words, but spoken new,
To eyes of wonder, bright and true.
You give your voice to bear the tale,
To make the brave knight never fail.
You roar the lion, squeak the mouse,
For ears within this cozy house.
Their pointed finger on the page,
Turns wisdom, rage, and joy to stage.
You see the story through their gaze—
A forest fresh with morning haze.

So first we read to fill our well,
A private, bookish, sacred spell.
Then we pour that treasure out,
In a shared, delighted shout.
We pass the torch of tale and rhyme,
Across the fragile stream of time.
For in the circle, soft and warm,
A new love for the book is born.
One page to find ourselves alone,
The next, to build a stepping stone.
The Lawnship
Created on August 22nd, 2025
Ragnar the Brave, a Viking so bold,
In a cardboard helm, a sight to behold.
His companion, loyal and true,
Was Ember the drake with scales green and blue.

They sailed their lawnship from morn till night,
Through deep, rolling waters by pale moonlight.
Ember soared high on silvery wings,
Told Ragnar tales of the songs she sings.

His yarn-beard flowed, his shield shone bright,
As Ember breathed smoke in the soft dawn light.
They fought fierce monsters that lurked in the deep,
While garden gnomes trembled, too scared to peep.

But when sun set, their play would cease,
The warrior and dragon found rest and peace.
Their voyage done, adventure withdrawn,
The mighty lawnship parked safe on the lawn.

For Ragnar the Brave was just seven years old,
And Ember the Drake was his cat, truth be told.

An Olfactory Trinity
Created on August 21st, 2025
The first proof is a secret, spun
From pulses slow and meals undone.
A personal, perfumed paradox,
A key designed for one sole's locks.
It blooms a warm and brief surprise
Beneath our own familiar skies—
A bodily released confession,
A fragrant, personal impression.
It’s there, then not—a ghostly trace,
A knowing smirk upon the face.
This gastro-symphonic event
Makes potpourri seem heaven-sent.

The second proof is marital,
A test of love that's not comparable.
For my true love, by fate's design,
Lacks any sense of scent—divine.
She cannot smell the fresh-cut pine,
The sour milk, the rose, or wine.
And so, with heart sincere and clear,
I tried to make the truth appear.
I said, "My dear, you don't smell good,"
And in the silent pause she stood.
She heard an insult, harsh and crude—
The proof that meaning can elude.

The third proof walks on two feet,
And makes the very air feel… sweet?
A pungent, potent, personal cloud,
Of musk that makes the meek feel cowed.
She haunts the space next to the water cooler,
Where musk mocks every molecule-er
Her name is Jean, it’s true,
And politeness is my debt and due.
But conversation seems a task
Behind my own supplied air mask.
So when our lonely pathways cross,
I calculate the social cost
And offer just, “Hi, Jean,” through filtered grace—
The proof I’ve braved this scented place.

Thus, these three smells, both bold and grand,
Are proof of life, close at hand.
From gut, from home, from workplace sphere,
They shout: You are alive and still here!

A Field in Bloom, A Sky in Pieces
Created on August 20th, 2025
The sun spills gold upon my woven threads,
A meadow’s loom where wildflowers spread.
Their breaths, a perfume, rise and sway,
Yet I, the meadow, feel roots begin to fray.

A tremor comes—not wind, but something frail—
Petals un-tethered, caught in a fleeting gale.
They are not insects, but fragments of a sky,
Stained-glass wings that forget to fall, and fly.

I watch them pulse, a breath I cannot hold,
A heart-thrum rhythm, tender, yet so old.
A clockwork wound by sunlight’s fleeting hand,
Their dance a tick that shifts my ancient sand.

These butterflies, my painted, trembling guests,
Stitch light through blades where shadows come to rest.
Their flutter—lace that dares the air to bend—
A whispered joy, too bright to comprehend.

The daisy, pale, extends her fragile lap,
A stage for velvet feet, a tender tap.
The violet, shy, her purple bruised by time,
Feels a brushstroke fade beneath the climb.

The poppy, bold no more, her petals fall,
Yet holds her heart for one last winged call.
I am the meadow, keeper of their pact,
Of nectar given, beauty brought intact.

But I am weary—my roots drink shallow now,
My blooms, my jewels, must soon take their bow.
A silent poem, of gold and fleeting red,
Lives in the pause where wing and flower wed.

My fading writes each line—no theft, but gift,
Each loosened root that lets the beauty drift.
I am the clockwork wound by my own decay,
The urgent spring that calls the wings to stay.

The Seagull
Created on August 20th, 2025
Behold the seagull, most un-gullible of birds,
Who speaks in screeches instead of words.
It practices sneaky snatch-craft at the beach,
Keeping your sandwich forever out of reach
Until it's in the gull's gullet,
Where it joins yesterday's mullet.

The seagull is afflicted with grab-biness,
A condition causing considerable scrabble-ness
In its table manners and social graces—
It steals french fries from babies' faces!
This feathered pickpocket shows no remorse
As it commits sandwich-theft by force.

I've observed that seagulls are tremendously foolish
In their assumption that all food is gullish—
That is, belonging to gulls by natural right.
They suffer from take-itude, day and night.

So should a gull consign your lunch to fate,
Don't curse the bird or fulminate—
You're not a victim, but a congregant
In the Church of the Endless Picnic Covenant.
Instead, consider yourself honored to participate
In the ancient rite they celebrate:
To communicate with the Spirit in the sky,
Who demands that all snacks learn to fly.




Author's Notes: contest Echoes and Distortions — Big Points - RoxanneChristiana - WC 164 words
Source Poem: "The Duck" by Ogden Nash - This poem is a reimagining of Nash's classic, adopting his signature style of humorous, mock-diagnostic observation, inventive hyphenated words (snatch-craft, grab-biness, take-itude), and a consistent AABB rhyme scheme. Where Nash playfully deconstructs the duck's anatomy, this piece applies the same lens to the seagull's brazen psychology, diagnosing its inherent greed as a condition from which they "suffer from take-itude."
The Diplomat
Created on August 19th, 2025
My purpose is a brief descent.
A wait in the dim, papery dark,
then the spin, the unspooling,
the dizzying fall from the roll.
The sudden tear of separation.
Now, I am a sheet. A shield.

The Crumple.
A practiced, hurried fist transforms me
into a soft weapon, a cloud.

Then, the plunge into the damp unknown,
the terrain I was made to meet.
I am the silent diplomat,
the gentle blotter of aftermath.
The only line of defense,
absorbing what the body has dismissed.

My pristine surface, briefly white.
I am the bargain struck with mess,
the necessary, fleeting touch
that spares the hand from truth.

A single, functional moment—
to dab, to press, to make clean—
is my entire existence.

Then, the final letting go.
The whirlpool's hungry pull.
A descent into the deep, dark plumbing,
taking the evidence with me.

I am used, I am soiled, I am gone.
Another takes my place on the roll,
waiting for their own brief descent.

Temporal Mutiny
Created on August 19th, 2025
The alarm clock has sparked a revolution,
Proclaiming "chaos" its grand solution.
It rallied the wall clock, timer, hourglass—
A rebel time union, a riotous mass.

Each clock now ticks to a rogue new beat,
Minutes scatter like dust in the street.
Three o’clock jumps before half past nine,
While noon just laughs and ignores the line.

The cuckoo bird won't leave his door,
He's laid twelve eggs upon the floor,
And now he's knitting tiny socks,
For all his future baby clocks.

The grandfather clock groans, "This is a mess!
Free time shouldn’t bring such distress!
You can’t let seconds roam willy-nilly—
Break the chains, but stay just a bit still-y."

I wake at "thirteen o’clock" every day,
As the bedside clock counts in a backward way.
The clocks have gone wild, Grandpa’s plea in my head,
I’m torn ‘twixt this chaos and craving some stead.

The Weight of Silence
Created on August 18th, 2025
I pack my grandmother’s funeral program— 
not because I loved her most,
but because I forgot to grieve her properly 
three years and three moves ago.

Folded twice, it weighs nothing 
in my palm, everything 
in my chest. The photo 
shows her hands, not her face— 
hands that taught me to hold 
sorrow like water, cupped 
but never contained. 

Phoenix waits with its angry sun,
subdivisions named after 
the cacti they paved over.
I’ll rent a studio apartment 
where the walls are thin enough 
to hear my neighbors’ lives 
seeping through—their laughter
a language I’ve forgotten,
joy stacked like borrowed chairs.

The program fits in my wallet 
next to a twenty-dollar bill 
and my expired California license— 
three paper rectangles 
measuring the density
of what I’m carrying forward. 

At the new place, I’ll pin it 
to the refrigerator 
beside take-out menus 
and appointment cards,
where it will yellow slowly 
under fluorescent light,
softening each day 
until it crumbles into forgiveness— 
the heaviest thing
I never learned to pack.

The Whisper's Shadow
Created on August 18th, 2025
There's a hint inside the hint,
A suggestion's shadow cast—
"The innuendo of the innuendo"
Echoes what was never asked.

Not the wink behind the word,
Not the mask, but the echo's glide—
The afterword of implication,
The tremor it leaves inside.

It lives in doubled doubles,
The subtext beneath subtext,
Where indirectness folds itself
Into nests of what comes next.

A meta-whisper, vapor-thin,
The shape of what was never said—
"The innuendo of the innuendo"
Now haunting in its stead.

It's the art of almost-speaking,
The pause that says it all,
The meaning caught in silence
When direct words would fall.

But sometimes even whispers
Can cut too sharp and deep—
"The innuendo of the innuendo"
Helps tender truths to keep.
The Territory of Maybe
Created on August 16th, 2025
There is a country between
the way you looked at me this morning—
coffee steam rising, your eyes
soft as apology—
and the way you look at me now:
flint striking steel across
the dinner table.

In that territory lives
everything that happened
and didn't happen,
the archaeology of hours
where love turned its back
just long enough
for rage to slip
through the unlocked door.

What lives there?
The unanswered text
at 2:47 PM.
The way you said "fine"
like a period
at the end of us.
The moment I chose
silence over truth,
thinking it would cost less.

Between tenderness and fury
stretches a no-man's land
where all our small betrayals
grow into something
with teeth,
where the distance
from pupil to pupil
becomes a canyon
I didn't know
we were digging
with our careful words,
our polite omissions,
our stubborn refusal
to say what we mean
when we still had time
to mean it.

Can We Be
Created on August 16th, 2025
I loved you in the quiet way
the moon loves the tide—
relentless, unseen, pulling
even when the shore resists.

You left like a season that forgot
to turn, leaving me stranded
in the wrong kind of light,
half-alive, half-eaten by shadows.

I begged the silence for answers,
but silence only knows
how to hold its own breath.
(You were always better at that.)

Rejection is a language
spoken in reverse—
I learned it fluently,
while you pretended not to hear.

And yet—
here I am, stitching my ribs back together,
not with thread, but with the memory
of how your laughter once fit between them.

I do not hate you.
I do not even blame you.
But tell me this—
when the dust settles,
when the ache becomes a story
instead of an open wound...

Can we be
the kind of strangers
who still leave flowers
on each other’s graves?

Ode to a Moose Who Thought Babysitting Was Easy
Created on August 14th, 2025
There once was a moose named Matilda the Magnificent,
Who thought babysitting twelve ducklings would be insignificant.
"How hard can it be?" she said with a snort,
"I'm enormous and they're the teeny-tiny sort!"

But ducklings, as everyone ought to know,
Are not the type to sit in a neat little row.
They splash and they dash and they make quite a hash,
Of everything tidy—in one mighty flash!

They slid down the banister, honking with glee,
While Matilda bellowed, "Come back here to me!"
But ducklings don't listen when fun is affoot,
They just keep on waddling—giving no hoot!

For when evening arrived and the chaos subsided,
Twelve sleepy ducklings around her resided.
And Matilda discovered, much to her surprise,
That chaos and love often wear the same disguise.

So if someone asks you to watch a small duck,
Remember Matilda and trust in your luck.
But if they ask you to watch a whole dozen,
Well, you might want to call up a cousin!
Author's Notes: contest Moose Babysits Ducklings - PinkFaerie5 - Word Count  168 - Inspiration for the technical aspect of this poem came from Ogden Nash.

The Keeper of Empty Rooms
Created on August 13th, 2025
I built a hotel in my chest
where all the leaving things go.

Check-in is easy—
no ID, no questions,
just the weight of their shadow
spilling across the front desk.

Some stay for minutes,
some for decades,
gnawing on the minibar of my ribs,
ordering room service
with a voice like frost on glass.

I change the sheets every morning,
fluff the pillows of my lungs,
scrub the bathroom tiles
until they shine like unclaimed promises.

The manager says a full house
weaves its own devotion.

The night auditor swears
ghosts tip better than the living.

I keep the lights on
and the doors unlocked,
not because I believe
in second chances—
but because I’ve tasted
the dust of abandonment
and prefer the ache
of a full house.
