The Architecture of Almost
Created on August 13th, 2025
We build with air, with breath, with sound—
each "soon" a brick, each "fine" a stone,
until the house is thick with walls
where every door is left unthrown.

"Love you," mortar, cheap and quick,
"Let’s talk soon," the lintel’s trick.
"How are you?" the rotted sill
that sags beneath the weight of still—

still waiting, still believing
the ghost of meaning lives here, grieving
in rooms where every "I’m okay"
paints shadows where the windows were.

We are the architects of absence,
drafting halls of acquiescence:
"No, go ahead," (though go means stay),
"It’s nothing," (swallowed whole as day

devours stars it swore to keep.
We name this kindness. Call it sleep.
But listen—how the structure sighs
when wind blows through its "not goodbyes."

The house will stand until it doesn’t.
The heart will bear until it won’t.
And when at last the roof gives way,
we’ll whisper "just another day"

into the dust where meaning fled—
not with thunder, but with "said."
The Stain of White
Created on August 13th, 2025
In shades of gray where choices dwell,
We cloak our lies as "kind" or "well,"
But paint them how we will, they stay—
False threads that fray the ties we sway.

Why white? As if the hue could cleanse
The rot where honest words commence.
No sugar coats a bitter core—
A lie remains, though masked as more.

"Your cooking shines!" (though salt forgot),
"You’ve aged like wine!" (when time has not).
Each "harmless" untruth, soft and sweet,
Is theft—of truth, of minds that meet.

You say, "But kindness justifies!"
Then watch the doubt grow in their eyes.
For even mercy, built on air,
Leaves ghosts of "What else isn’t there?"

The doctor lies to spare despair,
The friend hides faults to "show they care"—
But hearts, once burned by hollow light,
Grow cold to all that shines too bright.

Where’s the line? It’s drawn at none.
No lie forgives what’s left undone.
The smallest slit begins the fray—
No bind so firm it won’t betray.

So stand where light and shadows meet,
And choose the words that burn, not cheat.
For trust, once torn by "soft" disguise,
Is trust that never heals—but lies.
When Silence Becomes a Prison
Created on August 13th, 2025
They say that silence is golden bright,
But gold becomes a noose pulled tight—
A gilded chain around the throat
Of every undelivered note.

Silence is the assassin's blade
That cuts down truth before it's made,
The cancer growing in the bone
Of hearts that die while still alone.

It is the flood that drowns the scream,
The guillotine that ends the dream,
The poison poured in freedom's well,
The executioner's final bell.

In boardrooms where the vultures feast,
Silence feeds the hungry beast.
In homes where souls should dance and sing,
It clips the angel's broken wing.

The graveyard quiet after war,
When pride has locked and barred the door,
And mercy bleeds out on the floor
While silence stands guard evermore.

Children caged with muzzled mouths
Grow up as ghosts in haunted houses,
Their spirits crushed beneath the stone
Of voices turned to dust and bone.

The telephone becomes a tomb,
The door, a seal upon the room,
The questions buried six feet deep,
The truths that make the darkness weep.

Sometimes silence is the knife
That slowly bleeds away our life—
The vampire drinking at our veins,
The rust that eats away our chains.

So shatter silence like a curse,
Let chaos heal what quiet makes worse.
Better the storm than funeral calm,
Better the wound than numbing balm.
Guard Your Heart
Created on August 13th, 2025
Based on Proverbs 4:23: "Above all the things that you guard, safeguard your heart, for out of it are the sources of life."

In chambers deep where thoughts take flight,
Where dreams are born and fears take root,
Lives a garden, precious, bright—
The heart that bears life's sweetest fruit.

Guard it well from bitter seeds
That poisonous tongues might try to sow,
From envy's grasp and hatred's deeds
That make the springs of goodness slow.

For what we feed within this space
Will color every word we speak,
Will shape the love upon our face
And guide the kindness that we seek.

The heart's a wellspring, pure and true,
When tended with a careful hand,
But let corruption seep on through
And poison spreads throughout the land.

So build high walls of wisdom strong,
Let truth stand guard at every gate,
Choose carefully what you let along—
Your heart determines your whole fate.

Above all treasures, silver, gold,
Above all riches you might win,
Guard well the stories your heart holds,
For life itself flows from within.

Fever Dreams
Created on August 11th, 2025
The body hijacks its own blueprint,
sends the blood down strange tributaries,
temperature rising like a rebellious tide
that floods the careful architecture of days.

What was meant to be Tuesday
becomes a liquid landscape
where time moves sideways
and the ceiling breathes
in rhythms not your own.

You planned to finish the report,
call your mother, water the plants—
but fever has other plans,
pulls you into its humid kingdom
where thoughts grow moss
and memories drip like condensation
from windows you can't remember opening.

In this place between sleep and waking,
your mind takes the long way
through rooms that shouldn't exist,
conversations with people made of steam,
while somewhere distant
your phone buzzes with the abandoned world,
its urgency meaningless as rain
on a house made of clouds.

The thermometer holds its verdict:
this body is no longer governed
by schedules or intentions,
only by the ancient wisdom
of rest, of surrender,
of letting the current carry you
until you wash up, days later,
on the familiar shore of yourself.

Bell in the Current
Created on August 11th, 2025
You move through crowds like ancient tides,
Your thoughts as clear as morning glass,
While others rush with urgent strides,
You let the moment slowly pass.

No rigid spine distorts your form—
Instead, you bend with life's deep flow,
Through calm and wild, through peace and storm,
You pulse with rhythms few can know.

Your touch can heal or leave a sting,
Depending on the heart you meet,
Some fear the truth that you might bring,
Others find your presence sweet.

Translucent soul in murky seas,
You drift where instinct softly calls,
No anchor chains, no guarantees,
Just grace that rises, grace that falls.

The light shines through your honest core,
Revealing depths both strange and true—
A mystery the world adores,
Yet rarely understands like you.

The Heart’s Wild Mathematics
Created on August 10th, 2025
The heart speaks in crimson whispers,
its lessons written in the salt of tears
and the copper taste of first kisses.

It tells the brain: You are not the only oracle here.
Your maps of logic cannot chart
the geography of a mother's worry,
the fault lines of forgiveness,
the deep ocean currents of grief
that pull stronger than any tide table.

Listen, it drums against the cage of ribs,
there are truths that cannot be dissected
under your fluorescent laboratories—
how a stranger's smile can resurrect hope,
how the scent of rain on summer pavement
can unlock doors you didn't know existed.

The brain, with its filing cabinets of facts,
its careful calculations and measured responses,
learns slowly that some equations
have no solutions,
only the wild mathematics of feeling
where two plus two might equal infinity
or the square root of sorrow.

The heart teaches patience—
not the kind that waits for buses,
but the kind that holds space
for contradictions to coexist:
loving someone who has left,
finding joy in the midst of breaking,
being brave enough to be foolish.

It whispers: Sometimes the smartest thing
is to leap before you look,
to trust the parachute of intuition,
to let your compass spin wildly
until it finds its own magnetic north
in the warm pulse of another's hand.

And finally, drumming its ancient rhythm,
the heart teaches the brain its hardest lesson:
that wisdom isn't always knowing—
sometimes it's the courage
to feel everything fully,
to let the flood come
and trust that you will learn to swim
in waters deeper than reason,
more necessary than breath.
The Dream We Call Real
Created on August 9th, 2025
We wake each morning into dream,
Mistaking shadows for the beam
Of truth that never cracks the night—
Are certainties just tricks of light?

The ground we swear is firm and sweet
Shifts like dunes in windblown heat.
What we clutch dissolves like mist;
What we claim does not exist.

We build our homes and plant our trees,
Build our truths on trembling pleas,
While time dissolves each careful plan—
The dreamer dreams, but where's the man?

Behind the veil of what seems real
Are players we can barely feel,
Yet still convinced of what we show:
The heart's the only truth we know.

But sometimes, in the quiet space
Between two breaths, we find our place,
The dreamer stirs—truth shows its face,
Not waking, but remembering grace:

The hand that dreams, the dream it spins,
The place where every ending begins,
The sand, the star, the breath we take—
All one, before we fully wake.

Then dream, but with a looser hand,
Let tides erase your script in sand,
And love the ghost you can't undo—
The star that burns you through and through.

The Day You Escaped
Created on August 8th, 2025
Congratulations on surviving another lap
Around the sun without a major mishap!
Your parents probably thought "Oh no, what have we done?"
But here you are—their work is never done!

Your knees now crack like bubble wrap,
You grunt when rising from a nap,
Your back goes out more than you do,
But hey—you're still breathing, so woo-hoo!

Another year of questionable choices,
Of listening to your body's creaky voices,
Of pretending you remember people's names,
And losing constantly at memory games!

So raise a glass (but not too high—your shoulder!)
To getting magnificently older,
May your joints stay somewhat functional,
And your bladder remain punctual!

Unseen Echoes
Created on August 3rd, 2025
If a life fades in shadows deep,
With no one there its tale to keep,
Does silence drown the spark
That kindled in the dark?

Does frost at dawn still trace
The paths no gaze will chase?
Do petals burn with unseen flame
For eyes that never came?

The stars inscribe each fleeting breath—
Each laugh, each sigh, each brush with death.
Love whispered to the empty air
Still lingers, though none were there.

The man who broke his meager bread
For sparrows, murmuring as they fed,
Died alone where rivers bend—
Yet wings salute their silent friend.

Each life casts ripples through the tide,
In waves where quiet truths abide.
A stone’s soft fall shapes seas unknown,
Its echo carved in time’s own stone.

The tree that falls sings fierce and clear,
No ear required to overhear.
A life unwatched still claims its space—
Its pulse resounds in boundless grace.

No witness needs to mark its worth,
No voice to sing its fleeting birth.
Your question lights the truth’s soft flame:
It matters—ask, and name its name.
A Star Is Born
Created on August 3rd, 2025
The heart that stopped, eyes gently closed,
Mark when a precious life must go,
Its final breath, so soft, disposed—
Yet still the spirit starts to glow.

No grave can hold what souls contain,
Just flesh returns to earth again,
While essence rises, breaks night's seam—
A newborn star fulfills its dream.

Through cosmic void it finds its place,
A beacon born of love and grace,
Each life that ends gives birth above
To stellar fire, eternal love.

So when you whisper, "Where are you?"
Look skyward—there's your answer true:
That twinkling light, so bright, so free,
Is how they shine eternally.

Then sing it loud, let grief depart—
"Twinkle, twinkle, precious star,
Spirit bright, you are not far,
Love and grace shine from on high,
Dancing in the starlit sky!"

The Wheel of Seasons
Created on July 31st, 2025
When spring arrives with gentle hands of rain,
She wakes the earth from winter's silent sleep,
And bids the buried seeds their vigil keep
Until the sun dissolves each icy chain.
Then summer comes with gold and crimson stain,
Her bounty falls where autumn winds will reap
The harvest moon that makes the farmers weep
With joy to see such wealth from sun and rain.

But winter waits with patient, crystal breath
To blanket all beneath her silver veil,
When every leaf has danced its way to death
And bare-branched trees tell their eternal tale.
Through seasons' turn, through life and gentle death,
Love's cycle spins—and never once will fail.

Autumn’s Ache and Winter’s Silence
Created on July 30th, 2025
Between your empty chair at dawn
And mine that waits in time,
I wake to count each labored breath
Like coins I’ll never find.

Autumn lingers in the air—
its ache within my chest.
I touch where you once lay, then face
the mirror’s quiet theft.

Your absence carves my hollowed frame,
my lungs clutch shallow rest.
Death’s echo haunts our silent hall,
a chill I can’t suppress.

Yet here between these grieving walls
I trace your fading tune.
Your lamp still burns with trembling light—
its glow a waning moon.

I switch it off as dawn arrives—
the dark feels kinder yet.
The window holds soft light at noon,
where time and memory met.

Your voice still lingers in the glass,
my pulse frays thin with pain.
As evening bleeds to night I hold
this ache like April rain.

Losing you rehearsed my soul
to face its own embrace.
Winter’s hush now walks these halls—
a silence I retrace.

The Thorny Process of Letting Go
Created on July 29th, 2025
Like roots that drink from poisoned wells,
We clutch what cuts us to the bone—
The bramble-love that tears and swells,
The wounds we tend and call our own.

What is this pull, this buried need
That drags us back to dig and dwell
Where thorns we planted still take seed
In soil that reeks of old farewell?

Each day we tend with careful hands
What long ago should have died—
The bitter fruit our grief demands,
The thorns we've trained to grow inside.

We save the last voicemail's thread,
Each playback wearing thin the sound—
To delete would mean we've shed
The only proof that can be found.

Perhaps we fear the hollow space
Where quiet grows when fury dies,
The emptiness we'd have to face
If we stopped feeding old goodbyes.

But we can choose what seeds to sow—
The hands that claw must learn to peel.
What starves will wither; what we grow
Depends on what we no longer seal."

Shy

When Joy Becomes A Wound
Created on July 27th, 2025
She wears her smile like armor made of glass—
Transparent to the world, yet sharp enough
To cut those who dare look too close,
And slice herself when the pretending's rough.

"I'm fine," she says, the words a practiced prayer,
A mantra worn smooth by repetition's weight.
But fine is just another word for drowning
While standing perfectly still at heaven's gate.

Behind the brilliant curve of painted lips
Lives grief that has no name, no place to rest—
The kind of pain that eats you from within
While you smile wider at each small request.

The mother asks, "How was your day, my dear?"
The friend inquires, "You seem a little off."
She lifts the corners of her mouth like flags
Of surrender, soft but strong enough.

Each laugh becomes a tomb for silent screams,
Each "How are you?" a test she's learned to pass.
The mirror knows the truth she'll never tell:
That smiles can be the cruelest kind of mask.

At 3 AM, when darkness claims the night,
The smile dissolves like sugar in the rain.
She sits alone with all she cannot say,
With all the weight of unwitnessed pain.

But what if someone saw beyond the light?
What if the question came without conditions?
What if we learned to love the broken parts
And hold space for authentic admissions?

The bravest souls are those who dare to crack,
Who let their pain have voice, have form, have place—
Who understand that healing starts the moment
When we cease performing joy and show our face.

The Gilded Labyrinth of Almost-Truth
Created on July 27th, 2025
There is a moment, thin as breath,
When understanding meets its death—
Not in the absence of the mind,
But in the web too well-designed.

When scholars craft their towers tall
With words that glitter, shine, and fall
Like coins into a wishing well
That's deeper than their truths can tell.

The simple seed becomes a tree,
The tree becomes complexity,
But somewhere in its branching sprawl,
The roots forget why trees grow tall.

A child asks "Why?" with eyes so clear,
The expert's answer breeds more fear—
For in their need to sound profound,
They bury truth beneath the sound.

When jargon wraps around the core
Like ivy choking what came before,
When clarity becomes the cost
Of seeming wise while truth is lost.

The purple prose, the gilded thought,
The wisdom that can't be bought
Or sold or understood by those
Who need the truth the purple chose.

Purplexicity blooms when minds
Forget that light through darkness shines
Not by building walls of night,
But by letting in the light.

The deepest truths are often small,
The greatest minds don't need to crawl
Through mazes of their own design—
They speak in ways that clearly shine.

So complexity serves its place
When it unveils, not when it hides the face
Of understanding from our view—
The purple hue obscures what's true.

When knowledge kneels before its crown,
When wisdom seeks to write renown
In syllables the heart can't hold,
The story's warmth grows crystal cold.

The moment comes, that razor's edge,
When we step off wisdom's ledge
Into the purple-tinted mist
Where meaning can no longer exist.
Growing Older, Not Wiser
Created on July 27th, 2025
At seventeen, I thought my father knew everything.
At thirty-seven, I realized he'd been winging it
for decades, just louder and with more confidence
in his wrong answers.

Watch the man at the coffee shop
who's been coming here for twenty years,
still complaining about the same things,
still expecting different results.

My grandmother hoards forty years of grudges
in mason jars, perfectly preserved,
pulls them out during family dinners
like they're fine wine.

The boss who says "we've always done it this way"
while the company slowly bleeds money,
his experience a pair of blinders
he mistakes for vision.

Some people don't age like wine—
they age like milk left on the counter,
growing more sour, more rigid,
more certain they're right.

Real wisdom whispers, never shouts.
It says "I don't know" and means it.
It changes its mind when shown new facts
instead of building walls around old ones.

The smartest words I ever heard
came from a kid who asked her teacher,
"But what if we're wrong about that too?"—
while the adults nodded, uncomfortable.

Age gives you stories, not answers.
Time gives you chances, not guarantees.
And some people spend a lifetime
missing every lesson life tries to teach.
Author's Notes: When I was much younger, adults would say to me "The older you get the wiser you get" I am in my 70's and have yet to find that  true. If it were only that easy, the world would be in a much better place.
When Does a Push Become a Shove
Created on July 25th, 2025
The child's first steps—
a gentle push from behind,
or the first shove
toward inevitable falling?

We call it encouragement
when we nudge our daughters
toward mathematics,
but resistance when they push back
against our binary world.

The protestor's sign
pressed against the wind—
is this democracy's push
or tyranny's shove?
The line moves with the crowd's mood,
shifts with the news cycle,
bends with whoever holds the camera.

Your therapist pushes you
to examine the bruises
you've been calling love marks.
When does healing become violence?
When does silence become complicity?

The tech CEO pushes innovation—
connecting the world, he says,
as algorithms shove loneliness
deeper into teenage bedrooms,
as notifications push dopamine
until attention becomes addiction.

I pushed you away
the night you wanted to stay.
You called it self-sabotage.
I called it self-preservation.
Neither of us mentioned
the shove we both felt
when vulnerability
knocked at the door.

Consider the comma,
that small push
between clauses—
when does punctuation
become the shove
that changes meaning entirely?

The earth pushes up mountains
over millennia.
We call this creation.
The earth shoves continents apart
in seconds.
We call this destruction.
Same force,
different speed,
different story we tell ourselves
to sleep at night.

Your mother pushed you
toward piano lessons.
You shoved the bench back
after every recital
until music became the enemy.
Twenty years later,
you buy a keyboard,
wondering when rebellion
became regret.

The question isn't
where the line is drawn—
the question is
who holds the pencil,
who gets to erase it,
and why we pretend
there's a difference
between the pressure
we apply
and the pressure
we receive.

Perhaps push and shove
are the same motion
judged by who’s watching.
The real question is:
when did we stop asking
whose hand we’re holding—
and whose throat?
The Architecture of Affection
Created on July 25th, 2025
Is love the ancient code written in our bones,
A primal script that evolution owns?
Or is it learned, like language, line by line,
A craft we perfect with practice and time?

Watch the newborn grasp its mother's finger—
Is this love, or survival's whisper?
The oxytocin flooding neural streams,
Biology disguised as romantic dreams?

The mother bird who feeds her crying young,
Is tenderness truly what moves her tongue?
Or chemical commands she can't refuse,
Instinct wearing love's elaborate ruse?

Yet humans write sonnets to their beloved,
Build monuments to hearts that have been moved.
No peacock writes poetry to his mate,
No wolf composes ballads for his fate.

We choose to love the damaged and the strange,
The ones who offer no evolutionary exchange.
We love the dying, the distant, the opposed—
Choices that leave our genes decomposed.

The elderly couple, hand in weathered hand,
Their bodies beyond biology's demand,
Still choosing each other, day after day—
Is this instinct or something else at play?

Perhaps love is both the seed and the flower,
Instinct providing the initial power,
But consciousness crafting something more:
A choice to keep opening that primal door.

The mother's fierce protection—surely innate,
But her decision to forgive comes from a different gate.
The lover's desire—chemistry's sweet call,
But staying through hardship transcends it all.

Maybe we are animals learning to be human,
Our instincts the clay, our choices illumined
By something beyond mere survival's art—
The conscious cultivation of the heart.

For love requires what no instinct can teach:
The courage to be vulnerable, within reach
Of pain, of loss, of being truly known—
A risk that biology has never shown.

So perhaps love is instinct's wild beginning,
but grows beyond where evolution's winning—
where choice takes the wheel, where we decide
to love not because we must, but because we feel
something deeper than the need to survive:

The need to be beautifully, dangerously alive.
The Mirror We Refuse to Face
Created on July 25th, 2025
We are the judges with dirty hands,
Pointing fingers from our pedestals of sand,
Demanding perfection from every soul we meet
While our own flaws remain conveniently discreet.

You should be kinder, we declare with scorn,
While our own cruelty cuts like thorns.
You should be honest, we righteously demand,
As we weave lies with our other hand.

We expect heroes from ordinary men,
Saints from sinners, again and again.
But when we stumble, oh how quick we are
To find excuses, near and far.

"I was tired," "I was stressed," "I didn't know,"
"It's complicated," our defenses flow.
Yet when others fail in the very same way,
We offer no mercy, no understanding today.

Perhaps we project our own hidden shame,
Making others carry what we won't name.
Or maybe it's easier to critique from afar
Than to heal our own internal scars.

The mother who screams about bad parenting
While her own children suffer, silently smarting.
The politician who preaches about moral decay
While corruption guides his every play.

The friend who demands loyalty without question
But abandons ship at the first suggestion
Of inconvenience, of effort required—
Our hypocrisy leaves relationships expired.

So why do we hold others to impossible heights
While we stumble through our own dark nights?
Is it fear of facing our own reflection,
Of admitting our need for self-correction?

The truth is sharper than we'd like to confess:
We expect more because we accept our own mess.
We've made peace with our contradictions,
But offer others no such exemptions.

Until we learn to turn that mirror around,
To seek in ourselves what we think should be found,
We'll remain prisoners of our own creation—
Demanding from others our own transformation.
Why Do We Crave the Thorn?
Created on July 25th, 2025
We reach for roses—knowing well
how thorns will prick and petals fall.
Yet still we cup the bleeding palm,
and call the wound divine.

What gravity, what hidden law
compels the heart to break itself
against the cliffs of almost love,
yet whisper "once more"?

Is pain the tax on ecstasy?
The shadow where the light must dance?
For even ghosts of love outshine
the safest, barren air.

We drink the ache like vintage wine—
each sip a sweeter suffering.
The hands that bruise us feel like home,
the chaos, sacred art.

Perhaps the heart is not designed
for peace, but pilgrimage—
each scar a holy testament:
"I burned, I wept, I dared."

Love’s blade carves hollows in the chest,
then fills them up with stars.
What’s hollowed out will always hold
more light than solid stone.

So let the thorns stay in the stem,
let every kiss risk ruin.
The soul that never splits with grief
has never held the sun.
The Weight of Wings
Created on July 24th, 2025
They expect my pen to carve borders,
to bleed ink into manifestos.
But I long to trace the morning’s light
fracturing through my grandmother’s teacup—
golden, fragile, untouched by history’s weight.

Yet sugar dissolves into ash here,
each granule a village unmade,
each stir a protest I did not choose.

The editor’s voice crackles:
“Your words for the resistance issue.”
Outside, a bird sings—
not of freedom, not of cages,
just a thread of sound,
unraveling the sky’s silence.

My homeland nests in my syntax,
but my syntax carries me elsewhere—
a kite cut loose, drifting over alien fields.

In English, I am a stranger,
borrowing vowels like borrowed shoes,
slipping on their unfamiliar edges.
In Arabic, I am a ghost,
haunting my tongue’s eroded map,
words like stones too heavy to throw.

What is a poet without a wound?
What is a wound without its poet?
The question spins through my notebooks,
a threadless needle stitching air.

My mother tongue tastes of uprooted roots,
my adopted language fits like a stranger’s coat—
too tight, too loose,
whispering: You are not home.

Tonight, I try to write of stars,
weightless, innocent.
By dawn, they are eyes—
refugees’ gazes, border lights,
holes torn in the sky’s fragile cloth.

No poem escapes the weight of wings.
To breathe is to resist,
to write is to carry
a homeland that no longer holds you,
a verse that sings itself
into silence.

Author's Notes: contest 'The Horse Fell Off the Poem' - WhiteLioness - A contemporary response poem inspired by Darwish's themes - "The Horse Fell Off the Poem" by a Palestinian poet Mahmoud Darwish (Translated By Fady Joudah).
The Octosyllabic Octopus
Created on July 22nd, 2025
(Sung to the tune of "Hang Down Your Head Tom Dooley
by the Kingston Trio)

Hang down your arms, Octavius,
Hang down your arms and weep n' cry,
Your inky verse drowns readers' minds,
Your eight-beat lines make patience sigh.

You wrote your rhymes, Octavius,
In patterns tangled like seaweed strands,
Each line with eight unyielding beats—
A sunken song from murky lands.

The critics came, Octavius,
They shook their heads in weary dread,
"Your syllabic waves of ink
Have drowned our will to forge ahead."

Your publisher, Octavius,
He tore his hair in briny woe,
"These verses flood like tidal surges,
What can we do with your ink's flow?"

So hang your arms down, Octavius,
Let go your stubborn, squid-inked pen,
Try scrawling something fresh and free
Before you sink to depths again.

Yet still he writes, Octavius,
Beneath the dark and churning sea,
With eight arms spilling endless ink—
His doomed eight-beat poetry.

The Other Side of the Story
Created on July 22nd, 2025
The chicken crossed the road because
She heard McDonald's was hiring a new crew,
Thinking she'd beat them to the cause
And serve herself in nuggets, too.

But wait! She had a different plan—
She'd seen a dating app called "Cluck,"
Where roosters posed with protein cans
And promised her some feathered luck.

She waddled past the traffic light,
Her GPS was set to "BAWK,"
The rubber chicken squeaked in fright
As the real ones learned to jaywalk.

With dreams of work and love combined,
She thought she’d found her perfect fate.
But halfway through, she changed her mind—
KFC’s bright sign sealed her fate.

The moral here is crystal clear:
Just stick to laying eggs instead,
'Cause crossing roads brings trouble near—
You'll end up scorched and crispy-dead.

A Seat of Higher Learning
Created on July 19th, 2025
Your Hogwarts owl vanished, lost in the mail,
but fear not, young wizard—we won't let you
fail! No wand or charmed cauldron, no cloak
spun with glee, we've conjured you a toilet seat
for your bathroom's decree!

Now don't look so puzzled, it's perfectly
planned, this magical seat was crafted
by hand. It's enchanted with spells from our
finest prefects, and comes with some truly
crappy side effects.

This seat really runs on the finest
of magic, though sometimes results can be
quite dramatic. We aim to please with each
sitting spell, but this cushioned throne has
stories to tell.

The hinges may squeak with a mystical
tune, the seat might just glow beneath
a full moon. When nature calls and you
need to go, this seat knows exactly which way
the winds blow.

So welcome to magic, though not what you
sought, this toilet seat's worth more than you
thought. In the most important room when push
comes to shove, this seat will give you the support
that you love!

What Fills the Silence?
Created on July 14th, 2025
In silence where no breath disturbs the air,
No morning coffee steams beside the bed,
No gentle hand to brush away despair,
No whispered words where hope might once have fed.

The letters never written, thoughts unspoken,
The garden left to wither in the rain,
The promises that time has left unbroken
Because there is no soul to feel the pain.

No laughter echoing through empty halls,
No tears to wash the dust from windowsills,
No voice to answer when the darkness calls,
No heart to climb tomorrow's waiting hills.

The books remain unopened on the shelf,
Their stories trapped in bindings, sealed and still,
No eyes to read, no curious, searching self
To wonder what lies past the distant hill.

No hand to hold another's in the night,
No shoulder offered when the world grows cold,
No witness to the fading of the light,
No keeper of the stories left untold.

Yet in this void where nothing breathes or dreams,
Perhaps the question’s yours: what breaks this night?
What light do you bring to make the darkness bright?
Author's Notes: contest Why is life worth living - David John Brompton - This poem may not meet the contest intent/directions, but rather than forcing my reasons why life is worth living, I wanted to approach this from a different view point and then let you draw your own conclusion as to why it is worth living given the opposite to think about. The famous AllPoetry quote "Show don't Tell"
The Sideways Dance of Stars
Created on July 14th, 2025
Old Cancer clicks his claws at dusk—
not dawn—where tides exhale,
and Scorpio’s tail, long-rusted now,
still writes in salt and shale.
The starfish sing in minor keys
of ships that fled their trail,
while constellations, drunk on time,
stumble through their tale.

The Crab-Kings rode on liquid light,
their armies barnacle-born,
till Aries came, a wildfire ram,
and cracked them hoof by horn.
Now Gemini, those twin-tongued jesters,
mimic battles gone,
and Virgo counts the broken shields
but keeps the tally wrong.

O Leo, blind in furnace gold,
O Libra’s balanced blade—
the crab knows weight is just a wave
that bows things toward the shade.
The moon-child reels, her pirouettes
now salt, now sickle, now spade,
and every throne the tides erect
is sand, is script, is scade.

So let Sagittarius nock his dawn,
let Capricorn climb stone—
the sea will have its skeletons,
the sky its stolen throne.
For what’s a kingdom but a glint?
A sideways glance? A groan?
The stars dance on, and none remain
to name what they’ve outgrown.

Chief Blue Eyed Cat: A Nashian Tale of Feline Conquest
Created on July 13th, 2025
There once was a cat with cerulean sight so keen,
Who courted sweet Squaw Pieyed, perpetually preened,
Chief Blue Eyed Cat, sleek as silk, sly as sin,
Convinced her to marry with a purr and a grin.

The tribe held a wedding of feathers and fish,
Where Chief Blue Eyed Cat made his one sacred wish:
"I'll lead you to glory!" he meowed with great pride,
Then promptly curled up for a nap by his bride.

His first tribal meeting was held by the creek,
Where Chief Blue Eyed Cat gave a speech quite unique:
"Meow meow," said the Chief, "and furthermore, purr,"
The tribe nodded wisely and agreed with a stir.

Poor Pieyed, quite pie-eyed from the honeymoon brew,
Watched husband catch mice while the tribe's problems grew,
The Chief's only counsel was "Nap when you're tired,"
And "Fish taste much better when properly fired."

And that's how a cat with eyes blue as the sea,
Became the most powerful chief history'd see,
While poets like Nash would have chuckled with glee,
At this ridiculous feline monarchy.

The Arsonist's Hymn
Created on July 13th, 2025
I struck the match—the World said "No—
The Flame must serve the Steeple—"
But Fire whispered "Dare to glow,
Burn down their golden People—"

The Gallows watched me raise my spark,
The Crowd cried "Heresy!"
Yet when I lit the waiting Dark—
The Stars leaned down to see—

Oblivion's Feast! I fed the Air
With all their sacred Letters—
While Ashes danced like answered Prayer
On Chains turned to Fetters—

They called it Crime—I called it Song—
That leapt from my small Finger—
Till every Thou Shalt Not was gone—
And I—the lingered Singer—

Now let them build their Temples high
With Rules of frost and Sorrow—
My Sun still hangs against their Sky—
And burns their Tomorrow—

Just a Grain in the Sand
Created on July 13th, 2025
Among ten thousand grains, I feel alone—
A single speck in this vast field of stone,
Where countless others glimmer, sharp and bright,
Yet none can touch me in this endless night.

We drift in silence, countless though we are,
Each grain a world, each world a distant star—
The weight of solitude grows soft and brown,
As isolation's gravity pulls down.

Perhaps there's beauty in this crowded space,
Where multitudes can never touch a face—
The way we nestle close yet never meet,
Like strangers passing on a city street.

I taste the salt of tears I cannot shed,
Feel hollow spaces where connection fled—
These shifting masses whisper what they've known:
That even in the crowd, we die alone.

So let me rest among these shifting shores,
Where waves erase what permanence implores—
For in this gentle dissolution's art,
I find the tender breaking of my heart.
The Doggone Nap
Created on July 12th, 2025
There once was a hound hilariously dozy, 
In polka-dot pajamas, wrinkled, weird, and wild. 
He dreamed of bacon—soft and sweet and cozy, 
Then snored so loud, the curtains nearby smiled.

His flannel held the scent of squirrel and kibble,
A hint of bunny from his chasing spree. 
Each snort escaped in rhythmic, dreamy dribble,
As drool pooled near the shoes beside his knee.

The whiff of tennis balls lit up his brain, 
His tail began to thump in dreamland flight. 
A wheezy grin broke through the drool and strain— 
“This is the life!” he barked with delight. 

But then he woke—a twitch, a startled leap, 
The bacon’s scent had tugged him from his doze. 
Reality returned with smells so deep— 
He’d soaked his jammies. A soggy tale, I suppose.

Love on a Hot Tin Roof
Created on July 12th, 2025
You perch where the sun-struck shingles gleam,
a breath away from burning, yet you stay— 
the roof remembers every whispered dream,
each vow that slipped and melted in the day. 

The tin exhales the heat of yesteryears,
It sighs like echoes warping time’s cruel spine. 
You trace the scars of laughter, salted tears,
A love that stuck like tar to summer’s vine. 

The air is thick with longing, slow and sweet,
Like syrup on the skin it cannot soothe. 
The past curls up like smoke around your feet,
A ghost that lingers in the heat of truth. 

You press your palm to where the metal sings,
A tune of all the fires it could not keep. 
The evening hums with rust on broken wings,
And memory is just the art of sleep. 

So let the stars fall silent from the sky,
Like paint that flakes from promises once bright. 
You’ll hold what heat remains before you die,
Though dawn will bleach it all to bone-white light. 

And when the roof forgets the weight you bore,
When time has warped the nails and drowned the rain,
You’ll be the shadow stretched across its core— 
The love that burned, then cooled, and burned again.
Through Foggy Lenses
Created on July 12th, 2025
A spectacles' whisper, soft and low,
Through foggy lenses, truths appear.
The world's a blur, yet hearts bestow
Like clouds that hide what we hold dear.

I read the faces in the crowd,
With eyes that once saw every line.
Now I watch their movements proud,
A dance of shadows, not sunshine.

The stars, they shimmer, but I know
Each one's a face I've seen before.
The moon, a ghost, but I can show
The stories of its silent lore.

I laugh at how the world can fade,
But I see souls in every face.
Like autumn leaves that fall and stayed,
Yet hold the warmth of summer's grace.

The clock ticks on, but I still see
The hidden signs that time has made.
A joke, a sigh, a glance so free—
The old world's secrets now displayed.

I'm not just older, I'm wise and true,
In every blur, I find what's right.
The eyes may fail, but hearts break through
To see the world in its purest light.
Burning Letters
Created on July 6th, 2025
I write your name in gasoline,
a matchstick love—wild, unclean.
Each spark a word I shouldn’t say,
each flame a debt you’ll never pay.

You taught my heart to beg and break,
to gnash its teeth, to ache, to shake.
Now look what’s left: this ashen tongue,
a dirge of smoke where hymns were sung.

I loved you ruthless. Loved you blind.
Now fury festers, rots the rind
of every promise sweet and low.
You should have watched the embers glow.

But tonight, the wind is soft and slow—
it hums the tune I used to know.
The fire’s gone, the scars remain,
yet still the sky forgives the rain.

So take this ache, this twisted grace,
the ghost of us I can’t erase.
I’ll light no pyre, just one small spark—
enough to see you in the dark.
The Politician and the Lawyer: A Chat
Created on July 1st, 2025
The slippery pol with forked tongue so slick,
Met a sharp-suited shark—oh, the rhetoric!
With a handshake so firm and a smile so wide,
They circled like wolves, each stuffed with pride.

"My dear counselor," the spin doctor grinned,
"Justice is blind, but loopholes, my friend!"
The legal eagle scoffed, "Spare me your spiel,
I bill by the hour—let’s make a deal!"

"Ah!" cried the grandstander, "But voters adore me!
I promise the moon—and then some, you see!"
The ambulance chaser just rolled his eyes,
"Save the hot air—billable hours don’t lie!"

They quibbled and quibbled, each twisting the truth,
One weasel-worded, one proof-proof-proof!
Till the pollster’s puppet cried, "This is absurd!
Let’s blame the press—no one heard what we heard!"

The slick litigator just flashed a grin,
"Or sue them for slander—cha-ching! We win!"
So they backroom-dealt, shook hands (with a fee),
And left us all wondering—who’s screwing ME?

Moral: A pol and a lawyer walk into a bar…
…and somehow, we get the tab! Bizarre!

What the Water Remembers
Created on July 1st, 2025
She isn’t sleeping—
she’s listening to the crack in the sky
pour its slow confession
into the sea.

Her dress, half salt, half ghost,
floats like a surrender flag
or a bride who missed her vows.

The rocks cradle her, but barely—
as if she’s already less body
than boundary.

When the tide pulls,
it doesn’t tug at fabric,
but at the space between
almost and gone.

One day, the sky will finish breaking.
One day, the ocean will forget
which of them was the reflection.

Until then, she lies there—
not waiting, not weeping,
just being the question
the world isn’t ready
to ask aloud.

The Space You Left
Created on June 28th, 2025
I've stopped setting the table for two,
though the salt shaker still stands
where your hand would reach for it
without looking.

Your slippers have migrated
from their post by the door
to the closet's far corner -
not put away,
just resting.

I drink my coffee black now,
having forgotten the exact number of sugars
you took after all these years.
The spoon still tinks against the cup
the way it did when you stirred yours,
a sound I pretend not to hear.

The bed has become
an ocean of cold sheets,
and I navigate its waves
by starlight,
learning new constellations
in the ceiling's cracks.

Your favorite chair
has begun to forget the shape of you,
its cushions slowly rising
like bread dough proving overnight.
I don't correct them.

Sometimes I catch myself
humming that song you hated,
the one you'd groan at
and change the station.
Now I let it play to the end,
smiling at the empty passenger seat
where your protests should be.

The seasons turn without consulting me,
bringing back the flowers you planted
with their stubborn persistence.
They bloom as brightly
as if you were still here to see them,
which is how I know
you are.

Angels guard you, love surrounds,
Safe in whispers, softest sounds...

Go to sleep.
And dream so deep
The Weight of Whispered Things
Created on June 26th, 2025
Do words, once spoken, ever die?
Or do they linger in the air,
woven into the breath of the sky,
a whisper the wind cannot spare?

Do they travel beyond the ear
that was meant to hold them close—
do they slip into the atmosphere,
where secrets and storms compose?

What of the words sharp as knives,
hurled in haste, in rage, in pain—
do they cut the soul that thrives,
or do they rust in the rain?

And what of the tender, the true,
the confessions soft as dawn—
do they root where they were sown,
or are they carried on and on?

Perhaps the tongue is but a spark,
and speech a fire none can tame—
every syllable leaves its mark,
echoing, endless, in flame.

So speak—but know the air remembers,
and the world has ears unseen.
Your words may fade like dying embers,
or live where the winds careen.
What the Body Keeps Without Asking
Created on June 25th, 2025
Mercy is not what you think.
Not a dove released, not a judge’s pardon—
it’s the way a streetlamp hums
after the fight downstairs is over,
how the light stays on
even when no one looks up.

Emotions—
don’t call them storms or wildfires.
They’re the slow collapse of a porch step
under too many winters,
the way the wood remembers
every footfall but never cries out.

Then, the twist:
they are the same unfinished thing—
Mercy, the space between knife and skin
when the hand hesitates.
Emotions, the blood
already rushing to the cut
before it’s made.

Ask them apart if you want.
They won’t answer.
They’ve always been the echo
and the voice
shaping the same word.
Bean-Fueled Dreams
Created on June 25th, 2025
Old Wilbur found a farmer’s stash—
A tin of beans (the "Explosive" batch).
He scarfed them down with glee, then WHOOMP—
His belly blew him off his rump!

He zoomed up past the farmer’s shed,
A porky comet—bean-propelled red.
"I’M FLYING!" oinked the gassy hog—
Then froze. "Uh-oh. Was that a… fog?"

The barnyard watched in mixed delight
As Wilbur fart-steered left, then right…
Till one last toot—a dying squeak—
He landed thud… in Farmer’s creek.

Now Wilbur’s banned from legumes (sigh),
But winks: *"Next time? I’m trying rye.
And maybe jet packs." (Lesson learned:
All flights need thrust—not beans returned.)

The Great Gemstone
Created on June 24th, 2025
Another June yawns awake—
not with fanfare, but the quiet dread
of candles multiplying like uninvited guests.
The cake gleams, too sweet, too bright,
a edible tombstone for another year
I can’t quite remember losing.

My birthstone is alexandrite—
a chameleon gem, they say, shifting
from emerald to ruby under different skies.
I call it Alexandgreat, my private joke,
because what’s the point of a legacy
if you can’t laugh at its shadow?

People ask if I change color like the stone does.
Like I suddenly morph from mildly grumpy to aggressively cheerful
depending on the light.
No. Just… no.
I remain consistently me.
A slightly bewildered, cake-adjacent human.

Birthdays are a strange ritual, aren't they?
Everyone pretending you’re somehow newer than last year.
Like a fresh software update for your personality.
Please. I’ve had plenty of glitches already.

It's a day to celebrate, supposedly.
A chance to reflect on all I’ve accomplished.
Mostly, I reflect on how quickly time passes and
how many socks have disappeared in the dryer.

Today, I’ll wear green, not for luck,
but because it’s the closest color
to enough.

Requiem in Blue: A Ghazal for the Dolphins of Taiji
Created on June 24th, 2025
Each year the sea grows quieter, my love,
the singers vanish into red, my love.

The nets descend like broken promises—
what hymns still cling to empty nets, my love?

Their eyes hold galaxies we've never mapped,
yet we give them knives instead, my love.

The cove's embrace is colder than the deep—
why do we betray the wet, my love?

A mother's grief could drown the rising sun—
her child floats where light forgets, my love.

They say the hunt is tradition's right,
but what of mercy's debt, my love?

The tides still whisper never, never—
when will we learn what that means, my love?
Author's Notes: I wrote this poem to bring attention to Japan's annual  "Taiji dolphin drive hunting season". During this time hundreds of Dolphin are headed into a bay then brutally slaughtered, some captured and sold to amusement parks. If you haven't heard of this, please do some research and find out for yourself. This has to stop.

When Is Good Not Enough?
Created on June 23rd, 2025
The candle flickers, dutiful and bright,
but the night is a hungering throat—
it swallows the glow like a miser’s coin,
and good is a bridge with no moat.

The baker kneads his tender loaves,
golden as a morning sun,
but the starving dream of feasts untold—
good is a song left unsung.

The gardener tends his roses well,
each petal a whispered vow,
yet the passerby craves orchids rare—
good is an unclaimed bough.

The sailor steers by steadfast stars,
his compass true as bone,
but the storm demands a lighthouse beam—
good is a voice alone.

Then comes the child with mud-streaked hands,
offering a daisy, torn.
The world, so vast, still stops to hold
this good—enough, and more.
The Moon's Quiet Claim
Created on June 22nd, 2025
The Moon bends close—not touch, but near—
a breath that stirs the tide’s long ear.
The sea rolls over in its bed,
drawn by the light, by longing led.

All night it combs the waves with silver,
spilling where the shallows shiver,
and though the dawn will steal its glow,
the tide still sways the ache to go.

Oh, mark the phases, slow and sweet—
now crescent-shy, now swollen fleet—
how every pull is just a plea:
Stay close enough to hold, yet free.

And when the current ebbs at last,
the Moon lets go but still holds fast,
for even as the distance grows,
the ocean hums the tune it chose.

In the hush where drowned cathedrals weep,

a witch with a crown of knotted tides,
her laughter the groan of a sinking ship’s side.

Her skin is the sheen of oil on waves,
her nails, barnacle-sharp, carve sailors’ graves.
The nautilus curls where her heartbeat thrums,
a clockwork of echoes where lost breath hums.

The Cartographers
Created on June 21st, 2025
Your hands move like dusk settling on riverstones—
mapping the quiet places where light pools
in the hollow of my wrist,
the bend of my elbow,
those secret coves only moonrise knows.

You are no witch, but a scholar of luminous things—
your fingertips chart constellations
in the freckles scattered across my shoulders,
connecting them like villages
along some forgotten pilgrimage route.

We sit cross-legged beneath the patient stars,
passing a cup of silence between us,
its rim still warm from your lips.
The night air hums—
not with spells, but with the slow vibration
of a tuning fork struck long ago.

When you press your palm to mine,
the lines of our lifelines align—
an old story written twice,
in slightly different dialects.
The fire between us doesn't roar,
but glows like foxfire in damp wood—
a cool, persistent radiance
that outlasts the match.

At dawn, we'll fold our maps differently—
you'll carry the imprint of my pulse,
I'll remember the weight of your gaze,
and the space between us
will thrum with all the unsung verses
of this quiet, endless song.

Ode to the Butterfly
Created on June 16th, 2025
Oh, fluttering whisper of painted sky,
Dancing sunbeam, you float so high—
A living petal, by zephyrs blown,
Nature’s stained glass, in light half-grown.

You are joy, stitched into flight,
A poem the air itself must write.
The child who chases, wide-eyed, slack,
Sees magic wear a velvet back.

Yet—butterfly—what curious name!
As if your wings were spread with grain,
As if you’d flutter from the churn,
A dairy sprite—oh, take your turn!

(No milkweed ever squeezed you tight
Enough to justify that right.
But “bread-and-jam-wing”? “Sugar-sly”?
No—butterfly must stay. Good try.)

So drift, you flicker of soul set free,
Proof that the earth still dreams in glee.
The world sighs awe where’er you pass—
Tiny masterpiece… on legs of glass.

Love Is
Created on June 16th, 2025
Love is the sun’s first light, spilling gold on silent hills,
a touch like petals drifting, soft upon your skin.
It’s the quiet hum of dawn before the world awakes,
a secret only hearts can hear, a melody within.

Love is the smile that lingers, like a flame in winter’s hold,
a spark that needs no kindling, yet sets the soul ablaze.
It’s the brush of fingertips—a language without sound,
a map of constellations traced in each other’s gaze.

Love is the rain that falls when every storm has passed,
not thunder, not a tempest, but whispers on the leaves.
It’s the way the earth remembers every seed it’s ever grown,
the way the ocean keeps the moon though daylight pulls and grieves.

Love is the quiet hand that holds you in the dark,
not chains, but roots that deepen where no eyes can see.
It’s the breath between two words that need not be spoken,
the gravity of planets, spinning wild yet free.

Love is the echo in the hollow of your ribs,
a hymn the blood remembers, though the mind forgets the tune.
It’s the way the world grows brighter when you step into the room—
not fire, not a lightning strike, but the steady glow of the moon.
Author's Notes: Contest Tell me what love is?? - Lora Queen
The Inside, Joke
Created on June 15th, 2025
Brain:
“Analyze subject: Unreciprocated signals detected.
Obsessive thought patterns, clearly projected.”

Heart: (A dizzying rush of rhythm)
"Don't analyze! Just feel the light!
He smiled at me today! Everything feels right!"

Brain:
“‘Everything feels right’? A flawed assessment.
His smile was directed—at his left-hand placement."

Heart: (A fervent, almost desperate beat)
"It meant something more! I know it did!
A secret message, beautifully hid!”

Brain:
“Probability of shared meaning: negligible.
Data suggests a pattern – easily legible.”

Heart: (A quiet tremor, a flicker of doubt)
"You always bring me back to cold, hard ground.
Can't you just let the fantasy abound?"

Brain:
(A long pause—a subtle shift in processing)
“Fantasy…is efficient for motivation.
Perhaps a temporary suspension – calculation.”

Reflex
Created on June 14th, 2025
The knee kicks out, the pupils shrink—
the body acts before you think.
No pause for doubt, no room for grace,

a name once loved now burns like oil.

still flinching at his ringless hand.

Why Don't They Wonder
Created on June 12th, 2025
Grown-ups say the sky is blue,
but do they see it like I do?
Do they stop and stare and wonder why
the clouds are sheep that float so high?

Why do they walk so fast, so straight,
and never stop to investigate
the ants that march in tiny lines,
or how the sun on water shines?

Why do they say, "Don’t ask too much,"
when every rock and leaf and such
holds secrets big and wild and true—
like magic hiding in plain view?

And why, when someone’s not the same,
do grown-ups whisper, call them names?
Don’t they know that hearts can break
like glass that falls but can’t remake?

The world’s so wide! So bright! So new!
Why don’t they love it like I do?
Maybe they just… forgot to play,
and lost the wonder on the way.

The Green Edge
Created on June 10th, 2025
Dawn comes first as a rumor at the railyard—
gold licking the tracks to life,
while nightshift workers wipe their brows
and become, for one breath, gilded.

The river, though it swallows
rust and rain in equal measure,
still births herons from its mist—
each wingbeat a stubborn letter
in light's unfinished alphabet.

The bulldozer naps in its muddy cradle,
but already the wild grapevine
stitches its green thread
through the chain-link gospel,
mending what was torn.

Children at the bus stop
toss their laughter like seeds
between the cracks of commerce,
while the oldest oak on the block
leans down to slip an acorn
into the wind's pocket—
a small, planted fury.

What is hope but the earth's
relentless arithmetic?
Steel decays, but the sunflower
still knows east from west.

Even the scuffed soles
of the shoeless man
print their own compass rose
on the pavement—
his shadow stretching longer,
darker, surer,
as the sun climbs its ladder.

And when at last the streetlights
shudder into daytime silence,
you'll find the proof:
a blade of grass in the pavement's split lip,
its green edge
incandescent.

Author's Notes: Over the past several weeks I have been reading the works of both Dickenson and Whitman, both an inspiration to me. Today, I thought I would try and combine their styles together and I came up with this poem. Of course it doesn't do them justice but it does give me another reason to write and learn. Unless otherwise noted, image is my design.
The Hymn of a Hometown Frog
Created on June 10th, 2025
In murky depths where duckweed drifts,
A bullfrog guards his watery gifts.
No border drawn in ink or sand—
Just this log, and this stretch of land!

"Hark!" he booms with throaty cheer,
While gnats and moths all quake to hear,
"No foe shall breach these sacred bogs—
I am the swamp's most loyal frog!"

His flag? A cattail, frayed and tall,
His anthem? Spring peepers' call.
No taxes paid, no gold amassed,
Just fierce defense of breakfast passed.

He dueled a snake (and lost one toe),
Chased off a rat (then ate his foe).
No palace decked his humble reign—
Just sunset light on lily-pane.

So toast the unsung, green and proud,
Who love their patch of earth out loud.
Some serve with drums and bright parade,
Some serve with ribbits in the shade!

After Fifty Winters
Created on June 10th, 2025
We meet where the sidewalk ends,
where the old oak still stretches its arms
like a tired guardian between our past and now.

Your laugh—unchanged—
a wind chime in a silent house,
startling the dust from my memory.

I count the years in the lines
around your eyes, faint as pencil marks
on a well-loved map.

We were lightning once,
all split skies and reckless thunder,
but time has made us gentle—
two rivers that braided and parted,
now finding the same sea.

Your voice still fits the shape
of my listening,
like a key turning in a lock
I thought had rusted shut.

The evening stretches between us,
thin as the scar on your wrist
from that summer we swore
we’d never grow old.

And maybe we didn’t.
Maybe we’re still those wild flames,
just banked now,
embers beneath the ash.

When we hug, I smell the ghost
of your old perfume—
like lilacs and unfinished homework—
and for a moment, the years
are just leaves
brushing against the window,
waiting to be let in.
The Island Beckons
Created on June 8th, 2025
I anchored where the sea hums,
a lullaby of turquoise and time—
where the waves kneel
to kiss the shore,
their foamy lips whispering secrets
to the sand.

The sky, a bowl of endless blue,
holds no memory of clouds,
only the sun’s golden fingers
brushing my skin,
leaving warmth like a lover’s touch.

The trade winds come,
carrying the breath of jasmine—
a ghost of blossoms
dancing just beyond sight,
teasing the air
with promises of hidden groves.

The beach, untouched,
welcomes me like a returning tide.
The sand, fine as powdered silk,
yields beneath my feet,
each step a silent conversation
between wanderer and earth.

Here, the world is still,
save for the rustle of palm fronds
clapping softly in the breeze—
a slow, sacred applause
for the solitude,
for the sea,
for the moment
that belongs only to me.

Author's Notes: Contest Show Me - Snorthey5  - Unless otherwise noted, image is my design.
Winter's Quiet Song
Created on June 8th, 2025
The sky is a sheet of faded gray,
stretching thin over frosted fields—
a hush so deep, the earth forgets
to whisper back.

Bare branches etch their tangled prayers
into the wind’s cold, restless breath,
while somewhere, hidden under snow,
a seed dreams green.

Footsteps crunch the brittle dawn,
each one a fleeting signature
on time’s unblinking page—
soon erased.

The hearth hums low, a drowsy chant,
as shadows stretch and yawn,
swallowing the last thin light
of the year’s tired eye.

Night arrives with silver stars,
stitching silence to the land—
a quilt of dark and diamond bright,
winter’s lullaby.

Silence is Not Golden
Created on June 6th, 2025
Silence is a luxury we can't afford
In a world where our voices are reduced to a whisper
Where the loudest ones get attention, while the quiet ones are ignored
Where the noise of the system drowns out the whispers of our souls

We're taught to be still, to sit quietly, to observe and listen
But what about those who can't be still? Who are constantly on edge?
Who have been silenced too many times, who have lost their voice?

The pain in someone's eyes is a weight they carry alone
A desperation that comes with being a whisper in the wind

The quiet soul is battered by the noise of the world
But even in silence, there is strength

In holding space for those who've been silenced, we can create a new sound
A sound that's gentle, but unrelenting; a sound that's loud enough to be heard
A Lifeline
Created on June 6th, 2025
Hope is a flame that flickers in the dark
A spark that refuses to be extinguished

It's the whispered promise of a better tomorrow

The gentle breeze that rustles the leaves of our hearts

But hope can also be a weight that we bear
A burden that threatens to consume us whole

It's the longing gaze that fixes on a distant shore

Yet still, we hold on to hope
Like a lifeline tossed out by a stranger in need

We cling to its fragile thread
And pray that it will be enough

Hope is not always easy to find
But sometimes, in the darkest of nights
It's the soft whisper of a friend's voice

The gentle touch that soothes our souls
Fractured Horizons
Created on June 4th, 2025
In the eye of the storm, they thrash about
like a leaf on a tornado's path
their world a maelstrom of contradictions
a swirling vortex of emotions, thoughts, and desires

Their mind is a maze of twisted corridors
narrow passageways that lead to dead ends
each step forward met with uncertainty
as if the ground beneath their feet was shifting sands

The din of distractions assaults their senses
a cacophony of notifications, demands, and expectations
their attention fragmented like shattered glass
scattered across a thousand disparate surfaces

Their breath comes in sharp gasps, as if they're running
their heart racing with anticipation, anxiety, and stress
each heartbeat a countdown to the next deadline
the next meeting, the next appointment, the next obligation

And yet, amidst this turmoil, they find moments of stillness
in the quiet eye of their own gaze, where the world slows down
a fleeting sense of peace that's lost as soon as it's found
leaving them gasping for air, scrambling to regain control.

Dolly's Morning Splash
Created on June 4th, 2025
I am Dolly, queen of the sparkling sea,
and dawn calls me to race the rising sun.
My fins flick, slicing waves that pirouette,
as I leap, a silver arc against the sky’s blush.

The coral reef hums, a city of color—
pink anemones sway, clownfish dart like giggles.
I whistle to my pod, “Catch me if you can!”
and we surge through seaweed, a twisting, twirling parade.

Sunlight scatters like jewels on the water’s skin,
cool currents kiss my sides, tickling as I spin.
A turtle cheers, slow and wise, from a sandy throne,
while starfish applaud with their silent, starry glow.

We chase a school of fish, their scales a silver storm,
my heart drums fast, alive with the ocean’s song.
Through a cave’s mouth, I glide, shadows dancing on stone,
and pop out to splash, spraying rainbows to the foam.

My pod’s clicks echo, a symphony of glee,
we weave tales of adventure, of waves and liberty.
In this playground of tides, I’m home, I’m free—
Dolly, the dolphin, splashing joy across the sea.

The Hollow Family
Created on June 3rd, 2025
A man, half his head gone,
skull a red-rimmed void,
stands beside a woman,
her chest a hollow circle,
where a heart should be—
her face calm, eyes blind to feeling.

A girl between them,
small hands holding their losses:
a brain, pink and tagged like a soldier’s,
a heart, red and pulsing.
She’s the keeper of what they lack—
mind that won’t think,
heart that won’t love.

A family of absences,
bound by what’s missing,
the child carrying their weight.
What is a family without thought, without love?
A shell, a void, a burden.
Yet her grip whispers hope—
to mend the broken,
fill the hollows,
teach them to live anew.

What is a family without thought, without feeling?
A shell, a void, a child’s burden.
Yet in her hands, there is a quiet plea—
to stitch them whole,
to fill the hollows with something new,
to teach them that even the broken
can learn to live again.

Ruffled Feathers
Created on June 3rd, 2025
I hold my morning brew tight, I say, I say,
This rooster’s angrier than a hen in a storm.
My red comb flares, steam rises like a train’s scream,
Glasses on, I squint at the world with a scowl.

Feathers ruffled, colors burst—red, green, gold—
Like a parade that took a wrong turn, boy.
I strut with a coffee cup in my wing’s grip,
Sippin’ slow, plottin’ against that noisy dawn.

That ol’ sun thinks it’s the king, I say, I say,
Wakin’ me up with its golden, loud fuss.
But I’m a bird with sass, grit, and style,
Foghorn fury in every cluck and crow I make.

Step back, you pesky chicks, give me room,
My brew’s too hot for your nonsense today.
This rooster’s got plans, got fire, got flair,
So watch out, world—I’m rulin’ this mornin’!

Swamp Strings
Created on June 1st, 2025
In the murky depths of a "Deliverance" swamp,
an alligator sits, banjo cradled in scaly arms.
His weathered overalls sag, patched with tales of mud,
a rocking chair sways like a lazy current under him.
"Dueling Banjos" drifts through the humid air,
a grin spreads wide, teeth glinting like jagged stars.

His tail curls, a slow ripple of green muscle,
little gator kin splash around, eager apprentices.
They bob in the shallow water, eyes wide with mischief,
mimicking his strums with clumsy, webbed hands.
A blade of grass dangles from his snout, a cigar of the wild,
humor bubbles up, a croak that echoes like a chuckle.

The night wraps around, a blanket of shadows and reeds,
he plays on, a swamp maestro with no need for rhythm.
Each note a splash, each pause a breath of the bayou,
a comical king reigning over this watery stage.
Author's Notes: 147 words, Contest What Ever This Cartoon Inspires You by PinkFaerie5, image created my me using ComfyUI and Photoshop.
Lost Without a Trace
Created on May 27th, 2025
His memory deceives him, his recollection gone.
He wakes in the shadows, though it’s truly dawn.
A women's soft voice asks, “How are you today?”
But he doesn’t know her, or what to say.

A thought sparks suddenly, from the fog in his mind.
He thinks, “I know this woman, and why she is so kind.”
But like a bullet, fleeting, ever so fast,
The thought dissolves, gone without a past.

He can no longer join his waning life,
He can't remember is family and wife.
He dwells in a house unfamiliar to him,
Without hope of ever belonging again.
Author's Notes: Have no idea how this came about, I was work on a different project minding my own business, when all of a sudden I got this urgent urge to write about dementia.
Eternal Stand: The Tree of Life
Created on May 27th, 2025
Where ceaseless waves carve cliffs in misty air,
The Tree of Life persists, its roots laid bare.
Clutching the edge where land and ocean strive,
It stands, a quiet force, fiercely alive.

What unseen strength upholds its weathered form?
Defying storms that rage and tides that swarm.
Its branches, gnarled, stretch toward a fleeting dawn,
A symbol of resolve where hope is drawn.

Through endless cycles, it endures, unswayed,
Its roots like truths in earth’s own heart arrayed.
No tempest breaks its silent, steadfast grip,
A paradox where life and loss equip.

Oh, Tree of Life, you speak to souls adrift,
Your stubborn stand a timeless, sacred gift.
On that wild shore where northern rains descend,
You root our fleeting dreams where time transcends.

Ode To Thrift
Created on May 26th, 2025
In the heart of simplicity, dwells a subtle art,
Thrifty by nature's choice, not burdened with excess cart.
Each dollar earned and saved with care,
Is worth more than gold in mid-air.

Banquets less splendid yet richly flavored so sweet
Decluttered home where solace and neatness meet.
Clothes worn well past seasons' tides
Speak volumes about thrift that abounds outside.

Value found within pages old and torn,
Time-worn toys carry tales since they were born.
Discarded trinkets radiate new gleam when repaired –
A testament to prudence which others have barely fared.

Not driven by consumerism’s ravenous beast -
Never lured into feasts for possession increase;
Satisfaction springs from depths deep inside -
Treasured wants over misdirecting pride,

Investing time akin to precious gemstone blue;
Saving moments lost amidst routine owing its due- 
Daily joys savored slowly like an age-old wine-
Oh how they shine! That quiet life of thine!

So here lies thy hymn thrifty through every line:
Cherishing the unpretentious quietly divine!
Effulgence permeating all dimensions thick & thin —
Praise on thee ' Thrift’, our modest winsome kin!
Author's Notes: Contest by Adrewlason prompt word thrifty
Age A Reflection
Created on May 26th, 2025
Fade, the vibrant colors of youth,
Succumb to soft shades of reality's truth.
In obsidian mirrors and silken strands gray,
We glimpse our dawn transition to day.

Skin once kissed by morning dewdrops light
Now tells tales in wrinkles' quiet night.
Eyes that sparkled with unclouded view
Begin a dance with time -- A rendezvous.

Yet within those echoes lives wisdom deep,
Life’s stories etched where fleeting moments seep,
Lessons learned from emerald years now past-
Our reflections imbued by shadows cast,

Silver threads woven through memory's loom--
Echoes of laughter fill life’s sunlit room
Mellowing fine like seasoned wood are we.
Age is not just decline; it's eternity free.

So celebrate then this journey long made-
Every sunset hue, all traces left in shade.
For age cloaks us not in gloominess plight -
It adorns us instead...in tender twilight.
The Living Poem
Created on May 26th, 2025
Underneath a sapphire sky,
Where the swift birds freely roam.
Beyond thick leaves, with cicadas' sighs-walls,
There lies our verdant home.

Breezy whispers waltz around,
Through trees dressed in emerald grain.
The sun parades its golden crown
And paints life over plain. 

Daylight yawns and moon awakes,
Crickets hum their twilight song.
With tales whispered by ancient lakes,
Awash stars where dreams belong.

Observe dear Earth’s eternal reel   
Unbound is nature's humble tome:
In serene silence we unveil 
The beauty of her living poem.
Open Seasame
Created on April 18th, 2025
On Sesame Street, where friends all play,
A little gate blocked Charlie's way.
He tugged and pushed with a worried frown,
“I can’t get through—I’m feeling down.”

Big Bird saw Charlie's  drooping face,
“Let’s help our friend find a happy place!”
Staring with hopeful heart,
“Open Sesame,” he tried to start.

Elmo ran up, his giggle bright,
“Helping feels good—it’s warm like light!”
He held the gate with tiny paws,
Pushing hard for Charlie's  cause.

Cookie Monster munched a treat nearby,
“Me help, too!” he said with a sigh.
He shared a cookie, soft and sweet,
To give Charlie strength for his weary feet.

Oscar the Grouch, though grumbly he seems,
Hides a soft heart beneath his schemes.
He nudged the gate with a dusty broom,
While singing his "trashy" tune.

Ernie played his saxophone so fun,
While Bert said, “Teamwork gets it done!”
They pulled together, side by side,
Making room for Charlie's  stride.

Zoe zoomed in, her tutu bold,
“Kindness opens gates, I’m told!”
She danced and twirled to lift the mood,
Showing Charlie that help is good.

The Count clapped loud, his numbers clear,
“One friend helps, two bring cheer!
Three kind hearts, four hands so strong,
Together we can not go wrong!”

With every push, the gate swung wide,
Charlie's  smile grew big with pride.
“Open Sesame!” they all did sing,
Kindness unlocks the happiest thing.

On Sesame Street, they learned that day,
Helping hands can light the way.
When someone’s stuck, just lend a hand,
Together, we make life so grand.

Questions for God
Created on April 13th, 2025
In cities of gloom where the lost wander blind,
I ask God, “Why’s truth so darn hard to find?”
Their steps echo grief through a fog thick with dread,
No map lights their path where despair’s coldly led. 

A father holds tight to his son’s fevered hand,
He prays, “Lord, why’s healing beyond my command?”
The night steals his breath, and the dawn brings no cure,
How’s faith meant to stand when such pain must endure? 

Two lovers once swore they’d forever be true,
I cry, “God, why’s love just a ghost passing through?”
Their promises fade into silence and ache,
What’s left when their hearts find no mend for the break? 

On streets cracked by hate, where the angry divide,
I plead, “Lord, why’s peace pushed so far to the side?”
Words clash like sharp swords, tearing hope’s fragile seam,
Who’ll stitch back the world to its long-cherished dream? 

A girl sits alone with a weight none can see,
I ask, “God, what shadow makes joy bend its knee?”
Her smile hides a storm that no comfort can still,
How’s light meant to spark on her soul’s darkened hill? 

Through this storm of questions, my cries pierce the sky,
I shout, “God, why’s torment the weight we can’t pry?”
If pain shapes our fate, is Your answer too far?
A voice shakes the dark: “I’m the hope in your scar.” 


The Ant and The Grasshopper
Created on April 13th, 2025
Ol’ Grasshopper, famed from Aesop’s old tale,
He’d hop and he’d fiddle, his face never pale.
No work did he do, just danced in the sun,
Thought winter’s grim chill would spoil all his fun.

He mocked the ants’ hustle, their piles of grain,
“Work’s for the boring!” he’d laugh with disdain.
But when snowflakes fell, his tunes turned to moans,
His belly growled loud, no food but cold stones.

He knocked on Ant’s door, with a shivering plea,
“Spare crumbs for a fool who played carelessly?”
Ant shook her head, but tossed him some bread,
“Plan for tomorrow, or hunger’s your dread.”

Grasshopper learned well, now he fiddles with care,
Saves half his sweet corn for the cold winter’s scare.
So chuckle we may at his hop-happy ways,
But prep for the future, or tough come the days.

Author's Notes: Just got finished read Aesop's Fable to my granddaughter and saw this contest. Thought probably not adult enough but since it came to me I thought I'd write it anyway.
The Weathered Windmill
Created on March 16th, 2025
Against a saffron twilight glow,
A windmill sags, its blades half-gone,
Where endless seasons ebb and flow,
Through hail, through heat, through dusk till dawn. 

Its wood, now bleached to ghostly gray,
Still reaches toward the waning light,
Though time has gnawed its strength away,
It stands unbowed through coming night.

What whispers might its splinters share,
Of reapers’ dreams and ripened grain?
Of summers thick with humid air,
Of barren years that brought no rain? 

Perhaps it spun as kinfolk laughed,
Then slowed as footsteps wandered far,
As battles scarred the land they graft,
As vows were sealed beneath a star. 

With every strained and creaking sweep,
It hums to skies in amber dressed,
“Though time my sails in tatters keep,
My spirit turns, by storms caressed.” 

I linger on its battered grace,
Where evening paints a tender sheen,
And see within its cracks a trace
A beauty deeper than serene.

For in its stance, I find my own,
A strength to stand though faults unwind,
To meet the gusts that I’ve outgrown,
And let my scars with truth align.
 
Yet as the light fades from the hills,
A truth shines clear as day grows dim
This windmill turns not of its will,
But shows which way the wind has been.

The Smile Room
Created on March 13th, 2025
Through crumbled walls,
silence seeps,
a rotted hallway stretching into shadow.
An open door yawns
above it, "The Smile Room"
scrawled in red paint,
faint, like a murmur
from something broken. 

Inside,
dingy, desolate,
no echo of life
just dust hanging heavy,
a tomb untouched by time. 

Was this a place for laughter,
comedians weaving joy from thin air?
Or something darker
hands pressing smiles onto faces,
teaching lips to curve
without reason, without soul? 

Did we forget how to smile?
Did we stumble here, blank-eyed,
relearning what was lost,
mimicking joy until it stuck? 

The air thickens.
Shadows twist along the walls.
A chill claws up
from the splintered floor.
What happened here,
what died behind this door? 

I step in.
Darkness bites the edges of my breath.
Dust swirls—ghosts of forgotten grins.
Then a flash—
a mirror ignites in the gloom. 

My face stares back,
hollow as the room,
a stranger carved in glass
wearing my skin like a lie. 

No jests, no lessons,
just the weight of realization:
I’m alive, alone,
and this room
is where joy came to scream its last.
