The Weaver's Game (Long)
Created on March 12th, 2025
In a forest where shadows twist and play,
A weaver sat by the break of day,
Her loom aglow with threads of fire,
Spinning fates with a quiet desire.
Her fingers danced, her eyes agleam,
Each knot a choice, each thread a dream. 

I stumbled upon her, lost in the haze,
Through brambles thick and a twilight maze,
Her voice like silk, she called me near,
"Come, child, see what I craft from fear."
She showed me a tapestry, vast and wide,
Where my life unfolded, a turbulent ride. 

Here I was born, a babe in the sun,
There I ran free, my laughter spun,
But then a shadow—a choice gone wrong—
A lover betrayed, a friend turned to song.
The threads turned red, then black as night,
A storm of grief, a withering blight. 

"Wait!" I cried, "Can this be undone?
Can you weave me a path where I’ve already won?"
Her smile was sharp, a crescent moon,
"Step into my game, I’ll grant your boon."
She pulled a thread, the air grew thin,
And the forest spun as I fell within. 

I stood in a meadow, my past erased,
A crown on my head, a smile on my face,
My lover returned, my friends all near,
The sun so bright, no trace of fear.
But the ground felt hollow, the sky too still,
A creeping dread, a whispering chill. 

I turned to run, but the meadow stretched wide,
No edge, no end, just an endless tide.
The weaver appeared, her laughter cold,
"You wanted a tale where you’re brave and bold,
But every thread comes with a cost,
A perfect life means a soul that’s lost." 

She snapped her fingers, the scene dissolved,
Back to the forest, my fears unresolved.
I seized her loom, I tore it apart,
"I’ll weave my own fate, I’ll follow my heart!"
The threads unraveled, the air turned black,
But then—a mirror stared right back. 

The weaver was me, her face my own,
Her voice my whisper, her craft my throne.
I’d spun my own cage, my fears, my game,
Trapped in a loop, no one to blame.
And as I screamed, the forest fell,
Replaced by a room, a sterile shell. 

A hospital bed, a beep so slow,
A coma’s grip, a mind’s cruel show.
The weaver, the forest, the threads of fate—
All dreams I wove while I lay in wait.
My family wept, their voices near,
"She’s gone," they said, "she’s no longer here." 



Author's Notes: Narrative poem -WC - 414
The Last Oak Leaf
Created on March 10th, 2025
An oak stood gnarled, bent and old,
A weary tree in autumn’s grip,
Rough bark revealed a tale untold,
A frame where life began to slip.
One leaf hung on, so frail and thin,
With all its might it gripped the bough,
Its green now gold, a fragile skin,
A whisper of what once was now. 

The dawn crept in with chilly air,
A mist lay thick on hill and plain,
The leaf shook soft, alone up there,
And fought against the season’s reign.
Its veins grew weak, its hold unsure,
Yet still it clung with quiet might,
A thread of hope, so small, so pure,
To hold its place in fading light. 

A breeze blew in, so soft, so kind,
Through frosted fields it hummed a tune,
It tugged the leaf, its will to bind,
And pulled it free by morning’s noon.
The leaf resisted, tried to stay,
But strength gave out, it couldn’t last,
It fell with grace that cool fall day,
The branch released its hold so fast. 

The air then caught it, light and free,
Above the river’s shining face,
It soared where water gleamed to see,
A dance through beauty’s wild embrace.
Past meadows bright with flower and weed,
A world alive in bloom and strife,
The leaf drifted on, its heart to bleed,
Still aching for its former life. 

With edges browned, its spark grew dim,
The wind now twirled it high and grand,
Its golden hue was growing slim,
A soul that sank in autumn’s hand.
The earth below rolled vast and wide,
Its journey long, its sorrow deep,
A silent wish it couldn’t hide,
For roots it lost and couldn’t keep. 

The breeze slowed down, the air grew still,
It dropped the leaf on shadowed ground,
A cold, dark spot beneath a hill,
Where quiet secrets wrap around.
No eye could see the twist unfold,
As under that oak it came to rest,
Its home reclaimed, so soft, so bold,
Back where its life had once been blessed. 

The Festival of Dusk and Dawn
Created on March 9th, 2025
The sun dips,
a Holi maestro spilling its palette,
saffron cascading,
a molten river kissing the earth goodbye.
Red flares soft,
a tiger’s breath glowing amber,
yielding to the sky’s next act.
Purple blooms,
a lotus unfurling in twilight’s embrace,
its velvet hush a bridge between worlds.
Orange twirls,
a ribbon of flame unwound,
tying the day’s last light to the horizon.
Pink drifts,
a sprinkle of rosewater mist,
settling gentle as a prayer.

Then the moon rises,
no mere shadow of the sun,
but a festival of its own hues.
Silver ignites,
a cascade of molten pearls,
washing the night in quiet radiance.
Ivory gleams,
a swan’s wing brushing the stars,
its soft arc a serenade to stillness.
Pale gold flickers,
a buttery thread woven through,
the sun’s echo cradled in lunar glow.
Blue whispers,
a sapphire veil thin as breath,
draping the moon in mystic calm.

The colors dance—
sunset’s riot softening into moonrise’s hymn,
a handover of pigments,
vibrant to serene,
a Holi sky where day and night entwine,
each shade a celebration,
each glow a gift.

Author's Notes: Contest - Festival of Colors
The Parable of the Party Pig
Created on March 8th, 2025
The barn was decked in pink and green,
A pig’s sweet sixteen to cheer,
Miss Porky twirled, a sight unseen,
Her Pork-ceanera was finally  here. 

The cows brought gifts of hay and grass,
The chickens clucked in tune,
She snorted loud, a sassy lass,
Beneath a big balloon.

Her tiara gleamed on bristly hair,
The goats all clapped their hooves,
She ate her cake without a care,
Ignoring barnyard rules. 

The rooster tried to cut the rug,
His dance was quite a flop,
Miss Porky laughed and gave a shrug,
Her party wouldn’t stop. 

But then the mud called out her name,
She ditched the gown and crown,
Dove in with glee, no hint of shame,
The guests all wore a frown. 

The moral here is plain to see,
Through glitter, glitz, and cheer,
Be true to you, just let it be,
Yourself’s what makes you dear. 

Author's Notes: Contest - Party Pig _ WC -146
Codedependent
Created on March 8th, 2025
Lines spill from my fingers,
a cascade of jagged edges,
each one a shard of something alive,
something that hums when the air is quiet. 

I chase the pulse of it,
a rhythm that loops too tight,
stack overflowing,
heap crumbling under its own weight. 

When it stops—
when the stream dries to dust—
my skin forgets its shape,
a variable unassigned,
drifting in null space. 

The world greys out,
a canvas locked in render freeze,
no breakpoints to catch the fall.
I claw at the silence,
searching for a thread to pull,
a call to splice into the void. 

It’s not the ink I crave,
not the glyphs themselves—
it’s the dance of their assembly,
the way they knot and unwind,
a fractal tease I can’t debug. 

Without it,
I’m a process orphaned,
spinning cycles with no root,
waiting for the signal
to fork me back to life.

Where the Sea Remembers
Created on March 6th, 2025
The moon calls,
and the sea rushes forward,
pressing itself against the shore,
warm, alive, unafraid.
That’s us when it’s good—
you reach for me,
I spill toward you,
everything certain,
everything close. 

But then you turn,
a shadow crossing your face,
and the tide pulls back,
leaving the sand cold,
exposed.
I stand there,
watching you recede,
wondering if you still feel it,
if we’re still us. 

The night stretches,
and I look up—
stars flicker through the dark,
small, steady breaths of light.
They whisper to me,
soft as a promise,
that you’re not gone,
that this quiet won’t last. 

The moon flares,
its pull a fierce command,
and the sea roars back,
crashing hard against the shore.
I feel you—
your hands, your heat,
tearing through the distance,
and I’m alive again,
drowning in you,
my heart thundering,
whole, wild, yours. 

Against the Drift - (Frickin Floppy Fish)
Created on March 5th, 2025
In the shallows, where the water runs low,
Something flops, a wild and messy pile.
Not smooth, not still, it smacks the air a blow,
Slapping hard on mud, fighting all the while. 

It twists like it doesn’t know how to stop,
Bouncing loud where the wet turns to dry.
The stream pulls back, not caring what it’s got,
But this thing keeps flopping, refusing to lie. 

Once, it ruled the deep, quick and bold,
Darting free where the currents play.
Now it flops in muck, weak and old,
Too tough to die, too worn to sway. 

Its eyes, cloudy but locked, face the dry,
Not big, not done, just holding tight.
Each smack a grunt, each jump a try,
A soaked mess that won’t give up the fight. 

The water moves on, not slowing down,
While this heap flaps, ragged and sore.
No big finish, no safe ground,
Just battling on, like it did before.

Author's Notes: Written for the contest Frickin Floppy Fish - Upon reading this title (Frickin Floppy Fish) I visioned salmon spawning and that became the poems inspiration
Whispers of the Unseen
Created on March 5th, 2025
Beneath the moon’s veiled gaze,
stars hum secrets no ear can catch,
their light threading through shadows
like threads of a forgotten dream.

The cats perch silent,
eyes glinting with riddles,
their tails curling into question marks
against the mushroom’s cryptic glow—
pillars of earth holding up the unknown.

Leaves fall, not in surrender,
but as messengers from a hidden realm,
carrying whispers of what lies beneath,
beneath the soil, beneath the skin of night.

The forest breathes in riddles,
its colors shifting like thoughts
too vast to name—
reds and golds swirling into darkness,
a tapestry woven by invisible hands.

Here, the unseen pulses,
a heartbeat in the stillness,
inviting me to step closer,
to unravel the mystery
that lives just beyond the reach of sight.
Ram-A-Lamb-A-Ding-Dong
Created on March 4th, 2025
In March, I saw a lamb so sweet,
With horns that made my heart skip a beat—
But wait, oh no, it’s not a lamb,
It’s a goat in a scam, pulling a sham!

I called it "Lambchop," soft and mild,
But those horns poked me—oh, so wild!
It bleated back, "I’m no soft fluff,
I’m a mountain king, so call my bluff!"

The fields all laughed at my mistake,
This "lamb" charged forth, for goodness’ sake!
With prancing hooves and a cheeky grin,
It’s a ram-a-lamb—a goofy win!

So here’s to March’s woolly surprise,
A horned "lamb" with mischievous eyes—
Next time I’ll look before I leap,
Or get butted by this woolly sneak!

Author's Notes: Poem created for the contest March Lamb - Image provided by host and credited to ArtSenya - WC  113
The Light that Stays
Created on March 4th, 2025
I saw it once, in the crack under the door,
a sliver of yellow pushing through the dark.
Not loud, not fireworks—just enough
to make me wait, to make me listen.

Some days, it’s the creak of the floorboards
at dusk, or the murmur of rain against the window.
You don’t chase it, not really—
it finds you when you forget to look.

I think it’s always there,
like the breath you don’t notice
until it steadies. Believe in that,
and the world cracks open, quiet as a seed.

So I’ll hold to the small things,
the creak, the murmur, the steady breath—
they remind me: you’re never lost,
and magic lingers for those who see.

Author's Notes: Poem created for the contest Believe in Magic - WC 116 - Image provided by host and credited to Crystal Heat and Soul.
The Wood Shaper's Song
Created on March 3rd, 2025
Beneath the rafters, old and brown,
Where shadows dance on timbered wall,
The craftsman toils, with furrowed brow,
His lathe hums softly, a steady call.

His beard, like snow on mountain peaks,
Falls over his apron, coarse and worn;
With knowing hands, that craft unique,
He shapes the wood, by labor sworn.

His hands caress each whirling grain,
The wood obeys his firm design;
Each cut, a whisper, soft and plain,
In every curve, his gifts align.

The tools around, in ordered line,
Bear witness to his steadfast art—
The knives, the chisels, sharp and fine,
Each piece a treasure in his heart.

His craft endures, a timeless flame,
Through years that test his steady hand;
With every turn, he stakes his claim,
A master of this wooden land.

Yet while he bends, with heart so true,
The scent of cedar fills the space,
The lathe's low song will see him through,
In every curve, his heart's embrace.
Author's Notes: Seeing this image brought me back to the 5th grade when we had to read "The Village Blacksmith" by Henry Wadsworth Longfellow. I am sure you can see his influence in this poem. WC 158 -This image was provided by the host of this contest.

bah, humbug!
Created on December 1st, 2024
The alarm clock rings – that fiendish thing,
A modern torture, clear and true.
My pillows mock the pain it brings,
As comfort bids its last adieu.

What fool invented morning time?
What creature thrives at break of day?
To rise before the coffee's prime –
A punishment, I dare to say!

My feet, they drag across cold floors,
Like chains that Marley's ghost once bore,
While sleep-drunk stumbling through my doors,
Each step a fresh new form of war.

So spare me all your chirping birds,
Your "good" before my "morning," pray.
Until that brew has been procured,
Keep all your cheerful thoughts at bay!

For in this state of pre-caffeine,
Like Scrooge among his ghostly fears,
The world's a bleak and bitter scene,
And joy itself draws bitter tears!

But lo! The coffee pot doth steam...
(Though transformation's not quite yet)
Until that first sip I redeem,
I curse this morning with regret!
Author's Notes: contest Contest of Choice - Three Options for Themes - PinkFaerie5 - Image credit Mgsnell- Word Count 153
The Tale of Elf and Self
Created on December 1st, 2024
The Elf on the shelf was quite content,
Watching children day and night,
Until a message heaven-sent
Revealed a startling sight.

A twin named Self appeared one day,
Much like him, yet unique,
Who had a different kind of way
Of making mischief peak.

While Elf reported naughty acts
To Santa's watchful eye,
Self shared some therapeutic facts
That made the children sigh:

"Your worth's not measured by your deeds,
Or presents 'neath the tree.
Your heart is all that Christmas needs;
Just be content to be!"

Poor Elf was quite confused by this,
His purpose now seemed vague.
Should children earn their Christmas bliss,
Or skip the nice-list plague?

The twist came Christmas Eve at last,
When Santa stopped to say:
"I sent Self here to help you cast
A kinder light each day.

Now Elf and Self work hand in hand,
Each morning, noon, and night,
Together helping kids understand
What makes their hearts shine bright.

Navajo Sacred Path of Light
Created on December 1st, 2024
In the east, where dawn first breaks,
Yoolgai  shines pure and white,
While sacred colors start to wake
On pathways of the light.

To southern skies where turquoise gleams,
Dootl'izh's sacred blue,
The rainbow's ancient flowing beams
Shine forth forever true.

In western gold, the yellow sun
Makes diichili glow,
As sacred colors softly run
Between above, below.

The northern darkness brings its peace
With bááshzhinii's might,
Where rainbow colors find release
Through starlit roads at night.

Upon this bridge of sacred sky
Between the worlds we knew,
The colors dance and multiply
In paths of sacred hue.

The Wind bears prayers as we gaze
Upon their rainbow trail,
Where Holy Ones in ancient days
Drew back the sacred veil.

Through four directions, bright and clear,
Their rainbow path descends,
Reminding us they're ever near
As heaven earthward bends.

The Deep Below
Created on November 23rd, 2024
Everything about him was old except his eyes,
They were the color of the sea,
Where stars wheeled through darkened skies,
And mysteries ran free.

The old man rowed far beyond all ships,
Where currents dance and sway,
Past where the deep horizon dips,
Into the breaking day.

The great fish moved so beautifully,
With such dignity and grace,
Too deep beneath the restless sea,
Too far to find his trace.

Pain does not matter to a man,
He thought through salted air,
As waves against the vessel ran,
And hope mixed with despair.

The fish is my brother, he said then,
But I must kill him true—
The line cuts deep through flesh again,
As brothers sometimes do.

Man is not made for defeat, he knew,
While stars moved through the night,
Yet something in the waters blue
Would test his will to fight.

A man can be destroyed but not defeated,
Like the sea that has no end—
Time and tide forever sheeted,
Neither foe nor friend.
Author's Notes: contest Blackout poems - SerpentineSky - Ernest Hemingway's "The Old Man and the Sea", 1952 - The Deep Below image ©mgsnell
The Living Page
Created on November 18th, 2024
A butterfly, like a magic page,
Danced where the meadow gently rolled,
Each wing-flick turning nature's stage
To stories waiting to unfold.

Each graceful swoop across the light
Released new words into the air,
While letters shimmered, swift and bright,
As morning's ink flowed free and fair.

I reached to catch the floating text,
My small hands trembling with the thrill,
Not knowing which word would come next
Upon this page that wouldn't still.

The sentences swirled high and low,
Beyond my eager, searching eyes,
This magic page continued flow,
Its story told in wing-swept skies.

Now when I spot a butterfly,
I see that same enchanted page,
Still writing tales as it glides by,
A magic that defies each age.

Lunar Serenade
Created on November 9th, 2024
Midnight's hush, where shadows play,
I lift my bow, begin to sway.
The moon ascends, my only queen,
Her light, my stage's silver sheen.

In solitude, my song unfurls...           
The violin's voice softly swirls;         
D minor's softness, just for you,
Rising clear against the blue.

G7's tender notes arise,
My offering to midnight skies.
Em7's melancholy plea
Between the moon and only me.             

F# and E, I draw them near,
A secret only you can hear;               
Ab and Gb whisper high—                   
A private concert in the sky.

The music builds with quiet grace
In this sacred, starlit space.
B and A, my bow takes flight;
G and F#, yours tonight.

In D major's triumph clear,
No other audience draws near.
F# and A, and C# and E—                   
This serenade for you, from me.

My violin sings pure and true;             
Each note a gift, my moon, for you.       
Em7's yearning fills the air
A duet we two now share.

And when the music fades to black,
A7’s sigh resonates back;               
The moon's sweet answer from above
Blessing this nocturnal love.

In  silence, strings grow still;       
The last notes floating on night's chill.
F and E, a soft goodbye,
Until tomorrow's lullaby.

Author's Notes: Contest Music to the moon - Symphony8 - Word count 200

And the Christmas faery's spirit takes flight.

You might just see her, a sparkling light.
For the Christmas faery is here to say,
"Merry Christmas, dear ones, in a magical way!"

Thank's! 'No Worries'
Created on November 1st, 2024
In this world of hollow courtesies,
I offer thanks, sincere and unrestrained,
You toss back "No worries!" with callous ease,
As if my gratitude were feigned or strained.

My words of appreciation, carefully weighed,
Deserve more than your flippant, trite retort,
Your casual dismissal leaves me dismayed,
As if my sentiment were naught but sport.

Is genuine thankfulness now passé?
A relic in this age of glib exchange?
If "no worries" is all you deign to say,
I'll reserve my thanks for those less strange.

Your nonchalance is grating, I confess,
Next time, expect no warmth from me, my friend,
I'll match your apathy with coolness, yes,
And let this dance of empty words now end.

Who Will Hear Our Cry?
Created on October 26th, 2024
What secrets lie in mind's dark maze,
Where wisdom sleeps through countless days?
Which path among these twisted ways
Will lead us through this shadowed haze?

How did two hearts, once joined as one,
Lose all the grace they'd proudly won?
Through love's maze now, separate they run,
Till passion's dance came all undone.

Why must bullets fly astray,
To steal an innocent child away?
What words can soothe a father's pain,
When senseless violence leaves such stain?

Can time mend what violence tears?
Who heals the wounds of passing years?
Through endless halls we searching go—
Who will answer? Do we know?

Like wanderers in fate's design,
Through corridors of space and time,
Beneath the city's ceaseless roar,
What silent truths do we ignore?

When screams are lost in neon light,
Who hears the whispers in the night?
Through mazes made of steel and stone,
Who guides us when we're lost, alone?

Could ancient tomes reveal the way?
Do hermits' cells hold light of day?
Might wisdom rest in solitude
Where silence speaks in sacred mood?

Is truth heard in wind's soft sigh?
Or written in the starlit sky?
When will we find the peace we seek?
Which voice of wisdom will finally speak?

Through labyrinths of doubt and fear,
Whose light will guide when none seems near?
Will heart's pulse lead us through the night,
Till dawn breaks with its answer bright?

When earthly voices fade away,
And mortal wisdom fails to say,
Could heaven's eternal, loving eyes
Be watching as we seek reply?

The Torch That Fades
Created on October 25th, 2024
In the dying embers of a fading light,
Democracy's last breath whispers through the night,
A once-vibrant flame, now flickers out of sight,
Leaving behind a legacy of freedom's fight.

The halls of power, where voices used to rise,
Now echo with the silence of compromised compromise,
The people's voice, a muffled whisper in the noise,
As truth is twisted, and lies are made the choice.

The Congress, once a beacon of hope and pride,
Now crumbles under the weight of special interests' tide,
The promises of change, the hopes of a brighter day,
All lost in the haze of money's corrupting sway.

The death of democracy, a mournful tolling bell,
Echoes through the chambers of a hollowed shell,
The people's trust, a bond that's broken and worn,
Leaves only tears and sorrow, and a future unborn.

But even as the darkness closes in,
A spark remains, a glimmer of hope within,
A testament to all that's been and will be,
A memory of freedom's call, that still whispered "be".

Let this requiem for democracy's passing be,
A reminder of the power of freedom's legacy,
Let us arise from the ashes of a dying dream,
And rebuild the foundations of a freer future, once again.

Unveiling Truth
Created on October 22nd, 2024
In the mirror, I saw a stranger, shrouded in layers of doubt,
A silhouette wrapped in expectation, of a life to figure out.
I wore masks like a second skin, painted smiles on their face,
Each flaw magnified, each whisper of truth, buried deep within my place.

I thought I was the whispers of others, their voices echoing in glee,
“Be this or that,” they’d insist, “Conform and you will see.”
Caught in the tangle of their dreams, I wandered, lost within the night,
Chasing shadows of who I should be, blind to my own light.

Life’s mirror cracked open one day, revealing a glimmer of grace,
A moment of stillness, like dawn breaking, in this bustling race.
I began to peel back the layers, the dust of doubts that had grown,
With each heartbeat, unraveling lies, rediscovering my own.

I wandered through memories, both painful and sweet,
Finding strength in the battles I fought, the places where I’d meet
The rawness of my being, unrefined but oh so real,
And the love in my laughter, the power in how I could feel.

With every step on this journey, I shed the weight of the past,
Learning that my worth isn’t measured by the shadows that I cast.
I realized I’m not the puppet of whispers or fleeting praise,
But a tapestry of moments, woven through time’s mysterious maze.

I found clarity in the quiet, in the poems that filled my soul,
In the acceptance of my journey, I learned to be whole.
The scars tell stories of resilience, of growing skin deep,
And the dreamhouse I was building was rooted in my sleep.

Now, I stand in the light, no longer clouded by fear,
A canvas of colors alive, embracing each inch near.
For I’m not just a shadow or a mask that I once wore,
I’m a spirit unbound and magnificently so much more.

In the echo of my laughter, in the strength of my embrace,
I found the beauty of my essence, the truth of my place.
No longer defined by illusions, or what others believe,
In the journey of becoming me, I’ve learned to truly see.

Whispers of the Witching Hour
Created on October 21st, 2024
At the midnight hour when shadows dance,
And jack-o'-lanterns cast their glance,
I found you standing 'neath the harvest moon,
Your costume sparkling like stars in June.

The witching hour had cast its spell,
As Autumn leaves around us fell.
Your fangs though false, with glimmer shone,
While furry ears marked me as mine.

Through cobwebbed paths and pumpkins glow,
Past children dressed as witches low,
We walked together, hand in hand,
Two souls who seemed to understand.

The church bells tolled their solemn chime,
As we defied both space and time.
Your laughter echoed sweet and clear,
Through veils between worlds, drawing near.

When morning threatened in the east,
And spirits fled their midnight feast,
You kept the mask but gave your heart,
A Halloween love never to depart.

Now every year when witches fly,
And black cats prowl beneath the sky,
I meet you at the midnight hour,
Beneath the old clock's haunted tower.

For love ignited under guise,
Like autumn stars in evening skies.
When midnight skies on Halloween,
Two hearts unveiled what love does mean.

With sparkly wings and magical things,

As tall as your smallest fay.

Blazing Saddles
Created on October 18th, 2024
Two middle-aged men with boyish grins,
Embarked on pranks that reeked of sins.
Armed with lighters, beans, and brew,
They sought a flame of greenish hue.

"On three," said one, "We'll light the gas!"
But timing's not their strongest class.
A rumble grew, then with a "BOOM!"
It shook the walls of every room.

Singed and startled, they stumbled out,
Their neighbors gawked and began to shout.
"What's that smell?" came concerned inquiries,
While our duo nursed their seared posteriors.

With pants ablaze and pride in tatters,
They learned why safety always matters.
For men who play with fire and flatulence,
May find themselves in need of ambulance!

So let this tale a warning be,
To those who'd test gastronomy:
Some childhood dares are best left alone,
Lest your behind becomes well-known!

Midnight Visitors
Created on October 17th, 2024
In dead of night, a sound so near,
Jolts me from slumber, gripped by fear.
What spectral force now haunts these halls?
What ghostly hand upon me calls?

With trembling hand, I light my way,
Down winding stairs, in shadows grey.
Each creak and groan, a scary sign,
Of horrors lurking by design.

My heart pounds fast, my breath grows short,
As shadows dance and fears contort.
What waits on ground floor?
A ghost? A ghoul? Or something more.

The noise grows louder as I near,
I gather courage, fight my fear.
With trembling hand, I reach the door,
And find a sight I can't ignore.

For there, beneath the moon so bright,
Stand little ones, a cheerful sight.
With bags held high and faces beaming,
"Trick or treat!" their voices streaming.

Small witches, goblins, and cartoon heroes,
Pirates, princesses, and tiny Nero's,
All giggling, skipping, full of glee,
No scary monsters, just kids I see.

My racing heart begins to slow,
As laughter fills the night's soft glow.
The fright that gripped me starts to fade,
Replaced by joy at this parade.

I chuckle at my foolish dread,
The monsters were all in my head.
For Halloween's true magic lies,
In children's smiles and sparkling eyes.

The Snowman's Metamorphosis
Created on October 17th, 2024
In winter's chill, he stood so white,
A snowman proud, a frosty sight.
But as the warm sun graced the land,
He changed, but not as we'd planned.

His coal-black eyes began to gleam,
His stick arms stretched, or so it seemed.
His snowy form, once round and plump,
Began to shift, to move, to jump.

No puddle formed beneath his base,
Instead, he grew a human face.
His carrot nose turned flesh and bone,
A marvel that was yet unknown.

The scarf unwound, became his hair,
His buttons changed to clothes to wear.
The snowman that we built with glee,
Transformed into humanity.

So next you build a man of snow,
Remember this tale and you'll know:
Sometimes when spring melts winter's art,
A snowman might just find his heart.

United in this slow ballet.

A Bittersweet Scent
Created on October 14th, 2024
The scent of Thanksgiving's warm delight
Swirls through the streets, filling my lonely night.
I sniff and snuff, trying to hold on tight
To memories that dance, like sparks in the light.

The aroma of roasted turkey, sage and thyme
Reminds me of family gatherings, at Thanksgiving time.
When rosemary whispered through rooms divine
And laughter echoed, like sweet mulled wine.

I remember Mom's cooking, a banquet so bright
Onions sizzling, garlic's pungent might.
My siblings and I, noses twitching with glee
Inhaling deeply the scents of our family tree.

But now, these aromas brings tears to my eyes
For all that's been lost, times bittersweet prize.
I think of Dad's cologne, as he carved with flair
And Mom's perfume, when I was feeling bare.

The memories come flooding back, in a sensory wave
Of butter-basted bird, and gravy I'd crave.
But with each whiff, my heart begins to pine
For the life I once had, now lost to time.

I think of Grandma's kitchen, her apron string-tied
"Gratitude is key," she would say with pride.
Her eyes would sparkle, like cloves in the night
As she shared her blessings, a fragrant delight.

The scent of roasting veggies, carrots caramelized
Reminds me of my own kid's joy, now realized.
There giggles echoing, as they sniffed with care
But now they're grown, leaving my house bare.

So I'll hold on tight to these olfactory cues
Let turkey and stuffing bring comforting news.
For though it brings pain, it also makes me whole
A potpourri of memories, to soothe my soul.

As I take a deep breath, savor each note
Of cranberry tartness and gravy in a boat.
I know that even in sorrow, there's still time to heal
Through the scents of Thanksgiving, emotions reveal.

Summer's fiery blaze she gently slows,

Is also the one that whispers through winter's night.

A season of transition, with love shining true.

For in her heart, we find a peaceful place,

Times Toll
Created on October 13th, 2024
As I'm walking along the warm sandy shore,
I hear laughter, something to explore.
A short distance in front I can see,
a young girl and her dog running towards me.

With the ebb of each wave a soothing melody plays,
A rhythm she knows well as she dances with the waves.
Her smile is contagious, it brightens up the sight,
Like a comet's trail on a dark summers night.

In this peaceful scene, time stands still and slow,
While her laughter is carried on by the breeze's gentle blow.
I smile, feeling grateful in seeing this sight,
As the sun dips low, casting a warm, golden light.

A surge of emotions rise up from the deep,
Some which I've tried to forget but just had to keep.
Of times long ago when I was young and free,
Without any worries to bother me.

Sadness creeps in, a tear trickles down my cheek,
Whishing I was younger and my memories complete.
I to ran with my own dog, on this very shore,
With unbridled joy, our spirits soared.

But now, the years have taken their toll,
Time's fragile thread, begins to unravel cold.
My steps are slow, my heart beats less fast,
As I behold this scene, that will forever last.

In this moment, as the sun dips low,
I'm filled with a sense of sadness and woe.
For in my hourglass, the last grains will soon be spent,
And this sweet joy, will be but a memory to relent.

A Light in the Shadows

In every heart, there's a wound that's hard to hide,
A scar that forms when love and trust divide.
We've all been hurt, we've all been worn down too,
But in our darkness, a spark of hope shines through.

We've felt the weight of silence, the sting of pain,
The ache of loneliness, the bitter taste of shame.
We've known the fear of unknowns, the dread of what's to come,
But in our hearts, a resilience whispers "you're not alone."

We've all been there, where shadows loom and fears collide,
Where doubts creep in like thieves, and fears take their ride.
But still we search for light, for solace and reprieve,
For a gentle breeze that soothes the heart, and helps us breathe.

A flame of hope that flickers bright, even in the darkest shade.

So let's hold on to this spark, this light that shines bright,

And though we'll stumble, though we'll fall to the ground

Autumn's Whispers
Created on October 11th, 2024
In the heart of the forest, where trees stand tall,
A mystery unfolds, like a secret whispered through it all.
The leaves, they seem to be talking, in hushed tones so low,
About some hidden truth, that only they know.

Most trees are still green, their foliage a vibrant sight,
But a few outcasts have changed, and burst into autumn's light.
Maples turn golden, oaks crimson, beeches scarlet too,
It's like the forest is painting, with colors anew.

I wander through the woods, where leaves crunch beneath my feet,
And wonder why these trees are changing, before they're due to meet their leafy beat.
Is it some kind of magic, that only nature can see?
Or just a weird and wonderful twist, in the seasons' wild spree?

Maybe it's the earth's way of reminding us, to slow down and appreciate,
The fleeting beauty of each moment, like a firefly's brief, shining state.
Or maybe it's just a surprise, from the forest's ancient heart,
A message that only whispers low, and plays a subtle part.

I'll take my time, and savor the scene,
As the sun sets low, and the leaves rustle with an autumnal dream.
The colors burning bright, like embers in the night,
Fueled by secrets, whispered through the forest's ancient light.

The Long Goodbye
Created on October 10th, 2024
The autumn of my life arrives
unannounced and unwelcome, like a thief
stealing warmth from the bones,
leaving only shadows to inhabit.

Memories congeal on the edges of perception,
like frost on a pane that has long been still.
The fire that once burned bright is reduced to embers,
a faint glow that flickers, then dies.

I am a traveler without a destination,
turning homeward through landscapes of regret.
Each step, a weight that presses down,
each breath, a reminder of the passage of time.

In this twilight, I search for meaning,
but find only fragments of what's been;
the shards of shattered dreams, the splinters of love
that once seemed whole and complete.

And yet, even in decay, there is beauty;
a softening of edges, a quieting of pain,
a surrender to the stillness that gathers
like a slow snowfall on an autumn's day.

In this twilight, I find my own pace;
a gentle slowing of the heart's wild chase.
The world outside recedes, and I am free
to face the shadows that have long been me.

My eyes, once bright with hope and desire,
now dimly see the paths that I've conspired to take,
to leave behind, to hold on tight to love, to loss,
to life's uncertain light.

And when my time is finally done,
I'll let go of the ghosts that have been won,
the ones that lingered in memories of old will
fade away like autumn leaves grown cold.

Things Aren't Quite What They Seem
Created on October 9th, 2024
There once was a pigeon named Pete,
Who thought he had hooves on his feet.
He’d gallop and neigh,
In a very bird way,
And prance down the bustling street.

He’d strut with a confident flair,
With feathers instead of fine hair.
He’d race with the breeze,
And perch in the trees,
Pretending he didn’t have a care.

He’d try to eat hay from a trough,
But seeds were his favorite stuff.
He’d whinny and coo,
Confusing the zoo,
And making the zookeepers laugh.

One day, a wise owl did say,
“Dear Pete, you’re a bird, not a bay.
Embrace who you are,
A pigeon, a star,
And soar in your own special way.”

So Pete, with a flap and a cheer,
Flew high, leaving all doubt and fear.
He’d still sometimes prance,
Given half a chance,
But now he knew he belonged here.

Autumn's Masquerade
Created on October 7th, 2024
In my town, autumn lurks 'round every bend,
A trickster god, making nature pretend.
Old oaks dress in reds that'd make cardinals stare,
While birches go blonde, as if they just didn't care.

The wind's gotten nippy, with teeth sharp and cool,
Whispering secrets learned in summer school.
Squirrels dash madly, their cheeks puffed with loot,
Hoarding acorns and nuts, winter's backup pursuit.

At Farmer Joe's patch, pumpkins sprawl plump and bright,
Sun-kissed orbs glowing orange in fading light.
Each gourd a promise of lanterns to come,
Of spicy pies and doorsteps made fun.

Nearby, apples dangle, red-cheeked and gleaming,
Their branches bent low, with sweetness teeming.
Crisp scents mingle - fruit, smoke, and spice,
A sensory brew of autumn's device.

Geese honk goodbye, sounding drunk as they fly,
Their V's more like scribbles across the paint-splattered sky.
Below, the hills roll out in a patchwork so bright,
As if autumn had spilled all its colors overnight.

Days shrink like my socks fresh out of the dryer,
As porch pumpkins flicker with mischievous fire.
Autumn reigns now, in all its splendid glory,
A beautiful disaster, telling summer's last story.

Marking the moments, wishing they’d last.

A fire’s soft glow, a flickering gaze,
Longing for warmth beneath the sky,
As the quiet night fills the world with haze.

Surely you Jest
Created on October 1st, 2024
At a table set brightly with mismatched delight,
The Mad Hatter poured tea, though it felt rather slight.
“Would you care for some treacle?” he asked with a grin,
While the Dormouse, quite sleepy, was dozing within.

“Draw treacle from water?” young Alice exclaimed,
“Are you mad or just silly? The thought should be famed!”
The Hatter chimed in, with a chuckle and fuss,
“Well, you draw from a well—not from well, it’s a plus!”

“Oh, you surely draw from the depths of your mind,
To conjure such nonsense, I’m wholly aligned!”
The Dormouse awoke with a yawn and a stretch,
“Muchness is all, in this world — that I sketch."

“Sketch? What do you mean?” Alice puzzled, bemused,
“Much of a muchness?” she added, confused.
“Indeed, we draw memories; we draw them with glee,
Like drawing a mouse with a trap on your knee!”

But before she could ponder the meaning behind,
A new figure approached, with curiosity twined.
He was named Quirkle, with eyes full of jest,
“And I come with a riddle, for you all to test!”

With a grin like the Cheshire, and riddles a-plenty,
He spoke of contradictions, delightful yet risky.
“A hair of the dog that once wagged, now sits,
For what’s lost in the forest is found in the pits.”

The Hatter looked askance and the Dormouse did snore,
“Do you think we have time for this riddle in store?”
“Oh, but dear friends!” cried young Quirkle with glee,
“To find sense in nonsense is key, can’t you see?”

“Will I find what I seek in your riddles, dear friend?
Or will this chaotic tea party never quite end?”
With each twist of the words, Alice’s head spun,
For though she wanted clarity, she hardly had fun.

Success, she thought, must be buried beneath,
This eccentric exchange, like a riddle with teeth.
As the Dormouse was dunked in a whirlpool of tea,
Quirkle just chuckled, “It’s quite a sight, wouldn’t you agree?”

With a nudge of the teapot and a flick of his wrist,
He whispered, “The answers are there — you just missed.”
The tea party’s madness, so wild and profound,
Held lessons to savor, if one looked all around.

So Alice resolved, amid muchness of chat,
To seek hidden meanings in riddles like that.
With Quirkle beside her, a guide of sorts fine,
Her journey through Wonderland continued to twine.

Where puns danced like leaves and logic took flight,
In the land of the curious, day merged into night.
And amid all the chaos, with courage she’d tread,
To unlock all the wonders that lay just ahead.
Author's Notes: Written for the contest "The Mad Hatter's Tea Party"  - Image is my creation.
Making a Difference
Created on September 26th, 2024
My day was unscheduled, nothing was planned,
So I walked to the beach to put my feet in the sand.
The breeze was warm, gently guiding my way,
The gulls in the air inviting me to stay.

They beckoned me on to follow  their flight,
And along the way I saw a awful sight.
The shore was littered as far as one could see,
With hundreds of starfish cast from the sea.

The day before, a tempest raged, fierce and wild,
Tossing the waves and churning the sea, nature’s angry child.
The wind roared and ocean frothed, a dance of wrath and fear,
And many starfish lost their homes, their fate cruel and unclear.

With waves crashing down, they were scattered far from shore,
The fragile creatures tossed aside, cast out forevermore.
Yet in this chaos, hope arose, in a form so small and bright,
A little boy who saw their plight, determined to make things right.

And as I approached the boy, this question I did ask,
"For what purpose are you under taking this impossible task.
You can't make a difference, you can't save them all,
Time is your enemy and will be your downfall."

Looking back at me with a smile and a grin,
He turned and threw one more into the sea again.
Then with the satisfaction of a job well done,
He proudly stated with head held high, "I made a difference to that one!"

And it was in that moment that it was clear for me to see,
This little boy, was much wiser than me.
With his youthful innocence clearly on display,
He made a difference to each starfish he touched that day.

Pegasus and the Pearlescent Lake
Created on January 28th, 2024
With wings of wind-whipped moonbeams, he dances on the breeze,
Pegasus, the stardust stallion, where sky and slumber meet with ease.
His mane, a waterfall of whispers, trails a shimmering, silver wake,
As hooves of pearled quicksilver whisper secrets on the dawnlit lake.

He snorts a symphony of stardust, his eyes, twin pools of twilight's glow,
Flicker through the fields of fizzgiglets, where wishes bloom and never go.
His tail, a comet's silken banner, paints the heavens with delight,
It gilds the slumbering slumbertrees with pearlescent, moonlit light.

He gallops on the starlit meadows, a whisper on the dreamscape's hem,
A twinkle on the dream-kissed river, a lullaby for slumber's gem.
With every beat of moonlit feathers, he scatters laughter's chime,
And wakes the world with whispered secrets on wings of morning rhyme.

But hush! A glint in twilight's eye, a snicker in the breeze,
For Pegasus, the trickster prince, loves hiding in the trees.
He'll weave you dreams of spun stardust, then vanish in a wink,
Leaving starlight in his wake, and laughter on the brink.

So if you ever hear a whinny on a wind-kissed, velvet morn,
Or see a shadow dance in sunlight, on a cloud of silk reborn,
It might be Pegasus, the stardust steed, with secrets in his eyes,
A playful prince of moonlit mischief, in skies of sweet surprise..

The Ballad of Texas Red's Bathtub
Created on January 27th, 2024
Texas Red, a dusty soul, with eyes like sunlit sand,
Roped in a porcelain palace, a bathtub in the land.
No gilded throne, no velvet sheets, just copper gleaming bright,
But in that tub, with sudsy bliss, he bathed in pure delight.

He'd sing of mesas, canyons deep, and stars that blazed so bold,
His washboard rhythm, splashing tunes, a story to be told.
The desert wind would whisper secrets, through cactus spines so sharp,
As Texas Red, with soapy grin, would scrub away the scarp.

His boots, they'd rest upon the rim, his hat upon a hook,
And in that bathtub, free and clean, he'd find a hidden nook.
A solace from the sun's harsh glare, a haven from the trail,
Where dust and sweat would melt away, and dreams would softly sail.

One day, a rustle in the brush, a giggle in the air,
A curious sight, a petticoat, and eyes of sunlit hair.
Texas Red, he blushed and grinned, his sudsy song grew shy,
But in that bathtub, shared with her, beneath the desert sky,
He found a reason, warm and sweet, to keep that tub so grand,
For in its depths, he'd learned to love, not just the dusty land.

Zoo-nited States of DMV
Created on January 27th, 2024
In the DMV, a waiting room zoo,
Where sloths move faster than the queue.
A chorus of coughs and sniffles deep,
As hopeful drivers in line they steep.

A toddler wails, a symphony of woe,
His mother sighs, "Just five more numbers to go!"
A bored teen taps, his foot a restless beat,
Dreaming of freedom, on gasoline-fueled feet.

An elder dozes, wisdom in his beard,
Witnessed DMV dramas, absurd and unheard.
He'll outlast them all, this wrinkled old sage,
A champion of waiting, turning page by page.

But wait! A glimmer, a window's bright plea,
Please proceed to window three!
With shuffling steps and sighs that turn to cheers,
The chosen one to freedom disappears.

Yet here I sit awaiting my plight,
Hoping to hear "you may enter the light"
Author's Notes: Contest The Waiting Room - Tom Farrow  -  Word count: 131
Burns' Hidden Flame
Created on January 25th, 2024
Where sun-kissed ewes roam heather's lilac waves,
Burns croons of barley, whispers love it craves.
His heart, a stag scarred by a feathered sting,
Hunts solace in companionship's soft spring.

"Lass wi' eyes that dance like flames' hot breath," he sighs,
"Let embers of friendship in my spirit rise.
Past fires scorched trust to ash, a barren field,
Only fellowship's balm my wounds can yield."

Torn 'twixt yearning's sigh and passion's searing kiss,
He yearns for solace, shuns love's fierce abyss.
Friendship's mantle, draped on pain's bare face,
Conceals a smoldering ember's tender grace.

So raise a toast, let bagpipes wail and soar,
His songs, a tapestry of sun and winter's roar.
For in that guise, a hidden ember glows,
A whispering secret twilight only knows.

Whispers in the Globe
Created on January 23rd, 2024
In the hush of glass, suspended high,
A prisoner of breath, with muffled sigh,
Built his world, a fragile shell,
Where whispers weave a fearsome spell.

No sails unfurled, no oceans crossed,
No love's embrace, no battle lost.
He spun a web, of chilling light,
To shroud his heart from endless night.

A distant song, a melody unheard,
Where laughter blooms, he finds no word.
Their sunlit dance, a mocking flame,
As shadows coil, and whisper his name.

Ironclad fear, a chilling vice,
Holds him captive, pays a silent price.
He huddles close, a trembling form,
Dreading the storm, both cold and warm.

Frost-kissed domain, where hope grows bleak,
Can embers stir, a promise speak?
No sunlit hand, no joyous chime,
Can shatter walls, and claim his time.

The wind's soft song, a whisper near,
He feels the glass, the shifting fear.
Will he stay bound, a captive still,
Or face the fire, and climb the hill?

The fragile globe, on gossamer thread,
Hangs poised between the light and dread.
His trembling hand, a whispered plea,
Will he dare fly, or stay the sea?

Whispers in the Globe
Created on January 23rd, 2024
In a globe of glass, suspended high,
A man of fear, with muffled sigh,
Built his world, a fragile shell,
From worries spun, a fearsome spell.

No dreams pursued, no oceans charted,
No love's embrace, no victory harbored.
He spun a web, of fragile light,
To shield his heart, from doubts that bite.

Distant lives, a song unheard,
Where laughter lights, he finds no word.
Their smiles ignite, they dance, they roam,
While whispered phantoms claim his home.

But terror's grip, a chilling vice,
Holds him captive, pays no price.
He huddles close, a trembling form,
Afraid to weather life's warm storm.

Frost-kissed sphere, where dreams grow cold,
Will hope ignite, a story told?
No sunlit touch, no laughter's chime,
Can he escape, and claim his time?

The wind picks up, a whisper near,
He feels the walls, the shifting fear.
Will he stay bound, or take the flight?
The fragile orb hangs in the light.

The Breath of the Wild
Created on January 22nd, 2024
The wind whispers secrets through sun-dappled boughs,
A sigh, a swish, a sizzle-shuffle now.
Leaves, emerald whispers, pirouette and sway,
A ballet born of breezes all the day.

Babbling brooks, with pebbles click and clack,
A gurgling chorus, nature's rhythmic act.
Water whispers, tinkles, tumbles, sings,
A crystal symphony from hidden springs.

Then, thunderous hush, a curtain drawn aside,
A waterfall explodes in diamond tide.
Crashing, roaring, waters leap and churn,
A foamy frenzy, wild and newly born.

But wait, a hush, a whisper soft and low,
The torrent sighs, a lullaby aglow.
Wind lulls the leaves, the stream serenades,
And sunlight paints the forest in arcades.

Nature's orchestra, a symphony complete,
From rustling leaf to waterfall's retreat.
A chorus sung in whispers, sighs, and booms,
A lullaby of earth in verdant rooms.
Author's Notes: Contest Onomatopoeia poems - Whitnail
When You Broke Me: I Loved
Created on January 22nd, 2024
When you broke me, the fault line carved its way,
Through laughter's echo, through dreams we used to say.
Each shard a memory, sharp and glittering pain,
Yet in my chest, where love had lived, it bloomed again.

No fury scorched, no vengeful words took flight,
Just tear-stained whispers in the fading light.
For hate's a barren field, where nothing grows,
And I, my love, would rather tend a rose.

I see the shadows in your tired eyes,
The weight you carry, beneath your gentle guise.
And though it tore me, shattered piece by piece,
My love won't crumble, it finds release.

So let us tend these embers, hand in hand,
Though paths diverge, love's flame transcends the land.
For broken hearts, that choose to understand,
Find solace, not in blame, but in a gentler brand.

I'll hold your fragments, each one precious lore,
And whisper, "Love you still," when silence roars.
For though you broke me, my love did not depart,
It blooms anew, with an open, tender heart.
Cracker Catastrophe
Created on January 21st, 2024
Mr. Macaw: (Puffing out his chest)
"This cracker's mine, a beakful delight!
Green feathers and envy, take flight!"

Mr. Green: (Eyes narrowed)
"Not so fast, you squawking buffoon!
My turn to munch, beneath the moon!"

They squawk and they squabble, a feathery flurry,
But the cracker crumbles, in feathered hurry.

Both sigh, beaks empty, a comical sight,
"Next time, we'll share," they mutter, taking flight.

Whispers from the Heart
Created on January 15th, 2024

If you could hear me in your dreams, it wouldn't be a roar,
No trumpet blast, no crashing waves that pound upon the shore.
It wouldn't be a shout, no whispered plea, no siren's call,
But quiet music, like a brook that tumbles over thrall.

You'd hear the chime of laughter, soft and light, like wind in chimes,
A melody of sunshine, dancing through the passing times.
You'd hear the rustle of contentment, leaves adrift on summer breeze,
A sigh of peace, a whispered song, that sets your spirit free.

You'd hear the hum of understanding, deep and warm, a knowing light,
A hand that meets yours gently, through the velvet cloak of night.
You'd hear the echo of forgiveness, washing clean the wounds of strife,
A balm of trust, a whispered prayer, that mends the threads of life.

You'd hear the murmur of encouragement, a firefly's guiding spark,
A gentle nudge, a whispered hope, that sets your wings to mark.
You'd hear the roar of silent pride, a thunder in my beating chest,
For every dream you chase and conquer, every test you dare to crest.

You'd hear the whisper of "I love you," not a shout that tears the air,
But woven like a silken thread, through every note you share.
You'd hear the music of a bond, that time and distance cannot sever,
A lullaby of presence, whispering, "We'll be together, forever."

So if you listen closely, in the hush where dreams unfold,
You'll hear my heart speak volumes, more precious than pure gold.
For in the quiet symphony of sleep, where secrets gently bloom,
My love finds voice, a whispered song, just waiting for your room.

Wheelie Fun with Squeaks McSqueak
Created on January 13th, 2024
Squeaks McSqueak, the guinea pig bold,
With ears like sails and fur like gold,
Found a green scooter, sleek and fun,
Left by a giant, on the morning run!

He scampered aboard, paws on the grip,
Heart like a drum, whiskers a-flip,
Legs like pistons, strong and quick,
Green machine hummed, a happy click!

Through fields of clover, past buzzing bees,
Snuffling the breeze with a sniff and a wheeze,
Squirrels chit-chatted, birds in the sky,
Squeaks McSqueak zoomed, with a twinkle in his eye!

Over a bridge, tippy-toe slow,
Past sleepy cows with a moo-moo low,
Down a dusty lane, sun on his nose,
Squeaks' big adventure, where anything goes!

Home he scurried, tales to tell,
Of whizzing wheels and a magic spell,
Green scooter tucked, in hay so dry,
Squeaks McSqueak, with a wink and a sigh.

For in his dreams, the scooter flies,
Through rainbow clouds, reaching for the skies,
A tiny hero, brave and free,
Squeaks McSqueak, and wild machinery!

Breathing Life in Blue
Created on January 8th, 2024
The artist's brush, a cobalt stroke,
On canvas dreams... a world awoke.
He blew life into azure shades,
Where mountains leaned to kiss the blue cascades.

So blow, dear words, with every breath,
In shades of blue, defy the death,
For life's a tapestry, both bright and dim,
Where blues and blows are woven in.

Ballad of a Horses Horse
Created on January 7th, 2024
Dust devils whirled, his halo shone,
Young Jim on sun-baked earth alone.
Old Caballo, eyes like flame,
A fiery devil whispered name.

"Leave him be!" the bronc busters spat,
"No hand can tame that Bad Hoss!"
But Jim grinned wide, his heart alight,
"He craves a friend, not endless fight."

Pigeon-toed steps, a whispered plea,
Jim met the mustang's fiery glee.
His roughened palms on sun-kissed mane,
A silent peace that eased the pain.

No whip or spur, just trusting hand,
He spoke of meadows, cool and grand.
Caballo's snort, a trembling sigh,
His fiery heart began to lie.

Then sunrise burst, a dusty blur,
Man and beast, a dance to stir.
Hooves like thunder, laughter's gleam,
Across the plains, a sunlit dream.

He'd spin the devil, light as quail,
Then wink, "See, even fire can fail."
So next time eyes like embers glow,
Remember Jim, where wild hearts flow.

I may have taken too much liberty in writing this as it technically isn't about Horses (pl) but It immediately reminded me of a song sung my Marty Robbins "The Strawberry Roan" and I just went with it, it was too much fun to give up.
Grandpa's Rhyme
Created on January 4th, 2024
In Grandpa's lap, on creaky chair,
He'd weave a world with gentle flair.
"Hickory, dickory, dock," his voice would chime,
The clock's tall tale, it passed through time.

A tiny mouse, in rhyming verse,
Ascended timepiece, bold and terse.
Each bong! a step, a furry blur,
Down the face, adventures stir.

My tiny heart, with wonder filled,
Believed the clock with stories willed.
For days I watched, eyes wide and bright,
For that brave mouse, in morning light.

The hands they swept, the moments flew,
No whiskered scurry, ever true.
But Grandpa smiled, his eyes so kind,
"The magic whispers, in your mind."

Now years have passed, the pages turn,
The clock still ticks, lessons yearn.
In tales untold, and dreams alight,
Grandpa's voice, a guiding light.

So when I hear that rhyming beat,
Of hickory, dickory, down the street,
I see a boy, a clock, a dream,
The magic spun, a joyful gleam.

For even though the mouse won't show,
The time we shared, forever grows.
And in that rhyme, so sweet and small,
A love for stories, blooms for all.
Author's Notes: Contest Nursery Rhyme Poetry Contest {Part 4) - Theresa S  Image: Conceived in my mind and brought alive through photoshop and Stable Diffusion
'Because'
Created on January 4th, 2024
In hush of dusk, by embers slow,
I craved the secrets that did flow,
From depths of her obsidian eyes,
Where whispered truths and shadows rise.

"Why this, my love, why this you choose?"
My voice, a thread, the silence loose.
And then, she turned, a moonlit grace,
A single word upon her face:

"Because."

No grand decree, no thunderous rhyme,
Just that soft curve, that breath of time.
A tapestry of reasons wove,
In lines of laughter, words of love.

"Because the sunrise paints the hill,
"Because your hand feels warm and still."
"Because the rain speaks to the rose,
"Because the fire in darkness glows."

"Because we breathe, because we yearn,
"Because the stars in orbit turn."
"Because the soul has wings to rise,
And love's own language speaks in sighs."

A breath, a word, "because" alone,
A cosmos held in whispered tone.
No need for grand decree or tome,
Forever's echo finds its home

Let starlight hush doubt's fragile plea,
In boundless skies, where hearts agree.
On cosmic tapestry, souls entwine,
Love's echo, endless, yours and mine.
Viper's Lullaby
Created on January 1st, 2024
A new year dawns, viper's hiss and barbwire lulled to sleep,
Ink of war fades, silken dreams the open canvas steep.
No iron hearts, but palms like sun, where healing blossoms bloom,
No fear-etched lines, just stardust paths, chasing shadows from the room.

War drums slumber, hushed by trills on emerald wings,
Where echoes chilled, now wildflowers rise, bold love their message sings.
Beneath a sun that binds the torn, a butterfly takes flight,
A fragile peace on silken wings, bathed in forgiving light.
